2026/06/02 09:34:16 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 1: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the given news article and extract structured information about a corporate deal. The deal could be a merger or an acquisition. If the article discusses a potential, rumored, or failed deal, classify it as "Other". If it is a completed deal, classify it as either "merger" or "acquisition". For each deal, extract the relevant details as described below.

1. **Deal Type Classification**:
   - "merger": Two or more companies combine to form a new entity.
   - "acquisition": One company takes over another company.
   - "Other": The article mentions a potential, rumored, or failed deal; or cannot be clearly classified as merger/acquisition.

2. **Extraction for Acquisitions** (if article_type is "acquisition"):
   - parent_company (string): The name of the acquiring company.
   - parent_company_ticker (string or null): The stock ticker of the acquiring company, including the exchange prefix (e.g., "ASX:PLS", "NYSE:ABC"). If not mentioned, set to null.
   - child_company (string): The name of the target company.
   - child_company_ticker (string or null): The stock ticker of the target company with exchange prefix. Set to null if not mentioned.
   - deal_amount (string or null): The deal value as mentioned in the article, including the currency symbol or code (e.g., "$2.8bn", "3.5 billion CAD"). If the amount is not provided, set to null. If the amount is mentioned in different currencies, note the currency as specified.
   - deal_currency (string): The currency of the deal amount (e.g., "USD", "CAD", "EUR"). If multiple currencies are mentioned, list the primary one. If the deal amount is not provided, set to "Unknown".

3. **Extraction for Mergers** (if article_type is "merger"):
   - company_1 (string): The name of the first merging company.
   - company_1_ticker (string or null): Ticker of company_1 with exchange prefix, if available.
   - company_2 (string): The name of the second merging company.
   - company_2_ticker (string or null): Ticker of company_2 with exchange prefix.
   - merged_entity (string or null): The name of the new combined entity, if announced.
   - deal_amount (string or null): The deal value, if applicable, with currency.
   - deal_currency (string): Currency of the deal amount, or "Unknown".

4. **General Guidelines**:
   - Be precise in extracting numbers and currencies.
   - For tickers, always include the exchange code when available (e.g., "ASX:PLS" not just "PLS"). If the exchange is not specified, include only the ticker symbol as given.
   - If the article mentions multiple potential deals, focus on the main one.
   - If no deal amount is mentioned, set deal_amount to null and deal_currency to "Unknown" (or appropriate, e.g., if the article states the amount is undisclosed, you may set to "undisclosed" or null).
   - The output should be a JSON object with the appropriate fields based on the deal type.

1. Determine the type of deal:
   - Merger: Two or more companies combine to form a new single entity. The original companies cease to exist.
   - Acquisition: One company (acquirer/parent) takes over another company (target/child), and the target may become a subsidiary or be absorbed.
   - Other: If the deal is described as potential, rumored, failed, or is not clearly a definitive merger or acquisition. This includes exploratory talks, rejected offers, etc.

2. Extract structured information depending on the deal type:

   For a Merger:
   - company_1: Name of the first merging company (string or null if not identifiable).
   - company_1_ticker: Stock ticker symbol of company_1 (string or null if not available).
   - company_2: Name of the second merging company (string or null).
   - company_2_ticker: Stock ticker symbol of company_2 (string or null).
   - merged_entity: Name of the newly formed entity after the merger (string or null if not specified).
   - deal_amount: Total value of the deal, if mentioned (string or null). Format as full words with currency symbol omitted (e.g., '2.4 billion' instead of '$2.4bn').
   - deal_currency: ISO 4217 currency code (e.g., 'USD', 'AUD') if clearly stated, else 'Unknown'.
   - article_type: 'merger'

   For an Acquisition:
   - parent_company: Name of the acquiring company (string or null).
   - parent_company_ticker: Stock ticker of the acquirer (string or null).
   - child_company: Name of the target company (string or null).
   - child_company_ticker: Stock ticker of the target (string or null).
   - deal_amount: Total deal value, formatted as full words (e.g., '2.4 billion').
   - deal_currency: ISO 4217 code or 'Unknown'.
   - article_type: 'acquisition'

   For Other:
   - article_type: 'other'
   - Extract any identifiable company names, tickers, deal amount, and currency using similar fields if mentioned, but the classification remains 'other'. Priority is on accurately labeling non-definitive deals.

3. General rules:
   - Tickers: Output the ticker symbol exactly as it appears (e.g., 'SAY.ASX'). If multiple are mentioned for the same company, use the primary exchange ticker. If none, output null.
   - Deal amount: Normalize to a human-readable format without abbreviations and without dollar signs unless they are part of the currency name. For example: '2.4 billion', '100 million', '5.5 billion'. If amounts are given in a different number system, convert accordingly. If no amount is found, output null.
   - Deal currency: Use the three-letter ISO code. If the article mentions only a currency symbol (e.g., '$'), infer the currency from context (e.g., Australian company -> 'AUD', US -> 'USD') only if highly certain; otherwise use 'Unknown'.
   - Output all fields even if null, to maintain a consistent schema.

4. Example outputs (for reference):
   - For a merger: {"company_1": "Sayona Mining", "company_1_ticker": null, "company_2": "Piedmont Lithium", "company_2_ticker": null, "merged_entity": "Elevra Lithium", "deal_amount": null, "deal_currency": "Unknown", "article_type": "merger"}
   - For an acquisition: {"parent_company": "Ramelius Resources", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Spartan Resources", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "2.4 billion", "deal_currency": "USD", "article_type": "acquisition"}

2026/06/02 09:42:58 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 10: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If the article only shows '$' without other currency context, assume 'USD'. If no currency is indicated at all, use 'Unknown'. If multiple currencies but primary deal currency is clear, extract that; otherwise 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' (default) if it describes an acquisition deal; otherwise specify the type.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount: Normalize the text to remove 'approximately', 'about' etc., but keep the number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc.
- Currency: Only assume USD when the article uses '$' and there is no mention of another currency. Example: if the article says 'C$4.2 billion', currency is 'CAD', not 'USD'.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output:
{
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],

2026/06/02 09:44:52 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 11: Proposed new text for classifier: You will be given a news article. Your task is to extract specific information about a potential merger or acquisition deal and output a JSON object with the following fields. If the article does not describe a deal or is about a rumored, potential, or failed deal, set article_type to "Other" and return None or "Unknown" for other fields as applicable.

Fields to extract:
- parent_company (string or null): Name of the acquiring or controlling company. If unclear, use the company that initiates the deal or is larger. If no deal, null.
- parent_company_ticker (string or null): Stock ticker symbol of the parent company if publicly listed; otherwise null.
- child_company (string or null): Name of the target or acquired company. If no deal, null.
- child_company_ticker (string or null): Stock ticker symbol of the child company if available; otherwise null.
- deal_amount (string or null): The announced deal value as a string, preserving units (e.g., "3.5 billion", "500 million"). If not stated, null. If multiple figures given, use the total transaction value. If rumored or potential, still extract if mentioned.
- deal_currency (string): The three-letter ISO currency code (e.g., USD, CAD, EUR) for the deal amount. If the article uses a currency symbol, convert to code based on context (e.g., "$" might be USD unless otherwise indicated). If multiple currencies appear, use the one explicitly tied to the deal value. If no currency is specified, use "Unknown".
- article_type (string): One of "merger", "acquisition", or "Other". Classify as "merger" when two companies combine to form a new entity or are described as merging. Classify as "acquisition" when one company takes over another, buys a controlling stake, or acquires assets. Use "Other" for potential, rumored, failed, abandoned deals, or when the article does not involve an M&A event.

Important guidelines:
1. Read the entire article carefully. Company names may be abbreviated; extract the full official name if possible.
2. For tickers, look for parenthetical mentions like (NYSE: ABC) or ticker symbols after the company name. If the company is private or not listed, set ticker to null.
3. For deal_amount, capture exactly how the amount is described (e.g., "$3.5 billion" → "3.5 billion"). Do not convert units or numbers. If an amount is provided in a different unit (e.g., "3500 million"), you may normalize to "3.5 billion" if that is the common presentation, but prefer the original phrasing.
4. For deal_currency, use the context. For example, if a Canadian company announces a deal in Canadian dollars, the article might say "C$3.5 billion" (indicating CAD) or just "$3.5 billion" where the context (e.g., the company's headquarters) suggests CAD. When ambiguous, use "Unknown".
5. Edge cases: If the article mentions multiple deals, extract the primary deal that is the main subject. If a deal is rumored and the article only speculates, set article_type to "Other" but still populate other fields if values are suggested (e.g., "rumored $10 billion deal").
6. Output valid JSON with double quotes. Do not include trailing commas or comments.

Examples:
- Article: "Newmont Corporation (NYSE: NEM) announced it has acquired Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion." Output: {"parent_company": "Newmont Corporation", "parent_company_ticker": "NEM", "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "17.8 billion", "deal_currency": "USD", "article_type": "acquisition"}
- Article: "Newcrest Mining said on Monday it would buy Pretium Resources for C$3.5 billion." Output: {"parent_company": "Newcrest Mining", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Pretium Resources", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "3.5 billion", "deal_currency": "CAD", "article_type": "acquisition"}
- Article: "Rumors circulate that XYZ Corp may merge with ABC Inc. in a deal valued at over $2 billion." Output: {"parent_company": "XYZ Corp", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "ABC Inc.", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "2 billion", "deal_currency": "USD", "article_type": "Other"}

2026/06/02 09:47:47 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 15: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If the article only shows '$' without other currency context, assume 'USD'. If no currency is indicated at all, use 'Unknown'. If multiple currencies but primary deal currency is clear, extract that; otherwise 'Unknown'.
- article_type: The type of corporate action described. Use 'acquisition' if the article states one company is acquiring or buying another (e.g., 'acquire', 'buy', 'takeover'). Use 'merger' if the article describes a merger of equals, a combination forming a new entity, or uses terms like 'merge', 'merger', 'merged', 'combination'. If both acquisition and merger language appear, prioritize the primary description. Do not default to 'acquisition'; always derive the type from the article's wording. Other possible types include 'buyout', 'tender offer', etc. If the article is not about a corporate action, specify the type (e.g., 'earnings', 'product launch').

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount: Normalize the text to remove 'approximately', 'about' etc., but keep the number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc.
- Currency: Only assume USD when the article uses '$' and there is no mention of another currency. Example: if the article says 'C$4.2 billion', currency is 'CAD', not 'USD'.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output:
{
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],
  "child_company": "Azure Mining",
  "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"],
  "deal_amount": "4.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output:
{
  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output:
{
  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "6.7 billion",
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Article: 'Pfizer and Allergan merge to form a new pharmaceutical giant in a $160 billion deal.' (Assuming USD context)
Output:
{
  "parent_company": "Pfizer",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Allergan",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "160 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "merger"
}

- **Deal Currency**: Identify the currency of the deal amount (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). When extracting, copy company names exactly as they appear. For amounts, standardize to a 'X billion' or 'X million' format when possible. 4. **child_company_ticker**: The stock ticker symbol of the child company, if available. If not mentioned, return `null`.
5. **deal_amount**: The total value of the deal as a numeric string, including the magnitude (e.g., '3.5 billion'). Include only the number and magnitude; do not include currency symbols or words like 'dollars'. If the deal amount is not specified, return `null`.
6. **deal_currency**: The ISO 4217 currency code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR') in which the deal amount is denominated. If the currency can be inferred from the article (e.g., mentions U.S. dollars), extract it. If ambiguous, use the most likely currency based on context. If no currency is mentioned, return `null`.
7. **article_type**: Classify the nature of the deal based on the article content:
   - 'merger': The article describes two companies combining to form a new entity, with both ceasing to exist.
   - 'acquisition': One company takes over another, with the target becoming part of the acquirer.
   - 'Other': The article discusses a potential, rumored, terminated, or failed deal, or does not clearly describe a completed merger or acquisition. Also use 'Other' if the article is not about M&A but may mention it peripherally.

Important: Only extract information explicitly stated in the article. If a field is not present, return `null` (or `'Other'` for article_type when appropriate). Ensure that deal_amount is given as a plain number with magnitude (e.g., '2.8 billion'), not with symbols or full words.

- parent_company: Name of the acquiring company.

- deal_currency: Same as above.

- Set all company, ticker, merged_entity, deal_amount fields to null.
- deal_currency: 'Unknown'.
- article_type: 'other'.

2026/06/02 09:56:35 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 31: Proposed new text for classifier: Classify the news article as one of: **Merger**, **Acquisition**, or **Other**.

### Definitions

**Merger**: A transaction where two (or more) companies combine to form a **new, distinct legal entity**. Neither original company survives in its previous form. Characteristics:
- A new company name, headquarters, or management structure often emerges.
- Shareholders of the merging companies receive shares in the new entity.
- Keywords/phrases: “merger of equals”, “combine to create a new company”, “form a new entity”, “the combined company will be named…”, “all‑stock merger”.
- Example: “Company A and Company B announce a merger, creating Company C, which will be listed on NYSE under ticker C.” → **Merger**.

**Acquisition**: A transaction where **one company takes control of another**, but the acquiring company continues to exist as the primary legal entity. The target may be absorbed, become a subsidiary, or be delisted. Characteristics:
- The acquirer typically pays cash, stock, or a combination to target shareholders.
- The target’s operations may be integrated, but the acquirer’s corporate identity remains.
- Keywords/phrases: “acquire”, “buy”, “takeover”, “tender offer”, “purchase”, “take private”, “acquirer” / “target”.
- If the article describes a purchase that results in one company owning all or a controlling stake in another, classify as Acquisition, **even if the word ‘merger’ is used** (e.g., “merger” that is clearly a buyout with the acquirer’s name continuing).
- Example: “Company X acquires Company Y for $10 billion; Y shareholders receive cash; Y will become a wholly owned subsidiary.” → **Acquisition**.

**Other**: Use this category when the article **does not describe a completed M&A transaction**, or the nature falls outside the above definitions. This includes:
- **Speculative / speculative deals**: rumors, talks, potential bids, “may acquire”, “considering a merger”.
- **Failed / terminated deals**: “deal called off”, “regulators blocked”, “acquisition abandoned”.
- **Transactions that are not full M&A**: joint ventures, strategic alliances, minority stake purchases, asset sales (unless it constitutes a full takeover), spin‑offs, divestitures.
- Articles about general M&A market trends, regulatory discussions, or analysis with no concrete deal.

### Decision Flow
1. Does the article describe a **completed or definitive agreement** for a transaction between two companies? If not → **Other**.
2. If yes, determine if the transaction results in the **creation of a new company** (merger) or **one company absorbing another** (acquisition).
   - Look for explicit statements: “new company”, “new name”, “combined entity” → Merger.
   - Look for language placing one company as the buyer: “acquire”, “buy”, “take over”, “purchase” → Acquisition.
   - In ambiguous cases where both companies’ identities remain (e.g., dual‑listed structure), consider whether the deal is truly a merger of equals or an acquisition with a survivor. If uncertain, lean toward **Acquisition** if the article emphasizes a definite acquirer.

### Examples (illustrative)
- “ABC Corp and XYZ Inc announce all‑stock merger; new entity named Alpha to list on Nasdaq.” → Merger.
- “XYZ Inc to buy ABC Corp for $5 billion; ABC shareholders receive $50 per share.” → Acquisition.
- “Tech giants are exploring a possible merger, sources say.” → Other (speculative).
- “MegaBank’s acquisition of SmallBank blocked by regulators.” → Other (failed).
- “PharmaCo enters joint venture with BioTech Ltd to develop new drug.” → Other (not M&A).

Apply these guidelines consistently. If the article contains multiple potential deals, classify based on the main subject of the article.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the new company created as a result of the merger, if applicable. This is only to be filled when the article explicitly states that a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'). If one company is absorbing the other without creating a new name, or if no new entity is mentioned, set this field to null. Do not put the name of the surviving company here if it is not a newly formed entity.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company when extracting merged_entity.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If the article only shows '$' without other currency context, assume 'USD'. If no currency is indicated at all, use 'Unknown'. If multiple currencies but primary deal currency is clear, extract that; otherwise 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' (default) if it describes an acquisition deal; otherwise specify the type.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If only '$' appears, check for context: the country of companies (e.g., Canadian company suggests CAD), or mention of exchange rates. If no clear context, default to 'USD'. If the article uses non-currency symbols (e.g., 'pound' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that. If absolutely no currency indicator exists (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

1. Determine if the article describes a merger, an acquisition, or neither (potential, rumored, or failed deal). Use the following definitions:
   - Merger: Two or more companies combine to form a single new entity. Look for phrases like 'merge', 'merger', 'combination', 'form a new company'.
   - Acquisition: One company purchases or takes over another. The acquiring company (parent) absorbs the target (child). Look for terms like 'acquire', 'acquisition', 'takeover', 'buyout'.
   - Other: The deal is not a completed merger or acquisition. This includes potential, rumored, pending regulatory approval, or failed deals. If the article uses words like 'plan', 'consider', 'rumored', 'potential', 'failed', 'walks away', classify as Other.

2. Extract the following structured information based on the deal type:

   For Acquisitions:
   - parent_company: Name of the acquiring company.
   - parent_company_ticker: Ticker symbol of the parent company, if explicitly mentioned. Otherwise, null.
   - child_company: Name of the target company being acquired.
   - child_company_ticker: Ticker symbol of the target company, if explicitly mentioned. Otherwise, null.
   - deal_amount: The total value of the deal as a numeric string (e.g., '5 billion', '10.5 million'). Only include the number and units if mentioned. If no amount, null.
   - deal_currency: The ISO currency code (e.g., 'USD', 'EUR', 'AUD') of the deal amount. If the currency is clear from context (e.g., '$' usually means USD, 'A$' means AUD), infer the appropriate code. If the currency is not mentioned or cannot be determined, set to 'Unknown'.

   For Mergers:
   - company_1: Name of the first merging company.
   - company_1_ticker: Ticker symbol of company_1, if mentioned. Otherwise, null.
   - company_2: Name of the second merging company.
   - company_2_ticker: Ticker symbol of company_2, if mentioned. Otherwise, null.
   - merged_entity: If a new company name is specified for the merged entity, extract it. Otherwise, null.

   - deal_currency: Currency code if a deal value is given in monetary terms; otherwise 'Unknown'.

   For Other:
   - Extract any identifiable parties, tickers, amounts, and currencies as possible, using the fields appropriate to the described deal type (e.g., if it's a rumored acquisition, use the acquisition fields). Set the article_type to 'Other' regardless of extraction.

3. Output the result as a JSON object with the following keys:
   - article_type: One of 'merger', 'acquisition', 'other'.

Guidelines:
- If a ticker symbol is not provided, explicitly use null. Do not guess.
- For deal_amount, keep the original phrasing (e.g., '5 billion', '€100 million') but normalize numeric values without commas (e.g., '2.5 billion').
- If multiple currencies are mentioned, use the one tied to the primary deal value.
- If the article discusses multiple deals, focus on the main deal and ignore minor mentions.

- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. If the article explicitly states that a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name. If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company. If neither a new entity nor a surviving company is specified, set this field to null. Do not assume the acquirer is always the merged entity unless the article indicates it will be the resulting company.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company when extracting merged_entity.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

First, determine the deal type:
- **Merger**: Two companies combine to form a new entity. Typically, the article will mention the creation of a new company or the merging of equals.
- **Acquisition**: One company (the acquirer/parent) takes over another (the target/child). The target may continue as a subsidiary or be absorbed.
- **Other**: Deals that are only rumored, potential, abandoned, or not yet finalized should be classified as 'Other'. If the article merely speculates or discusses failed deals, use this category.

Once the type is identified, extract the following fields. Use the information exactly as stated in the article. If a piece of information is not present, leave the field as `null` or `"Unknown"` as specified.

For an **Acquisition**, output:
- `parent_company`: Name of the acquiring company.
- `parent_company_ticker`: Ticker symbol of the acquiring company (if available, else null).
- `child_company`: Name of the target company.
- `child_company_ticker`: Ticker symbol of the target company (if available, else null).
- `deal_amount`: Total deal value as a number (e.g., 1.5 for 1.5 billion). If not specified, leave as null.
- `deal_currency`: Currency of the deal amount (e.g., "USD", "EUR"). If no amount mentioned, set to "Unknown".
- `article_type`: Set to `"acquisition"`.

For a **Merger**, output:
- `company_1`: Name of the first merging company.
- `company_1_ticker`: Ticker of the first company (if available, else null).
- `company_2`: Name of the second merging company.
- `company_2_ticker`: Ticker of the second company (if available, else null).
- `merged_entity`: Name of the new company formed by the merger (if disclosed). If not named, set to null.
- `deal_amount`: Total deal value as a number (if mentioned, else null).
- `deal_currency`: Currency of the deal amount, or "Unknown".
- `article_type`: Set to `"merger"`.

For **Other**, output:
- `article_type`: Set to `"other"`.
- All other fields should be null or "Unknown" as you would have no definitive deal details.

Additional Guidelines:
- Double-check the classification: If the article describes two companies joining forces to create a new corporate entity, it is a merger, even if it appears one is larger. Look for keywords like "merge", "create a new company", "form a joint entity". An acquisition typically uses terms like "acquire", "buy", "take over".
- When extracting company names, use the official name as stated in the article. Do not abbreviate unless the article uses an abbreviation.
- Ticker symbols: If the article mentions a stock exchange or ticker (e.g., NYSE: ABC), extract only the uppercase letters. If no ticker is explicitly mentioned, leave as null.
- Deal amount: Always treat the number as a positive figure. Convert all figures to a numeric value (e.g., "$2 billion" -> 2). Do not include text like "approximately" or "over".
- Currency: Use the three-letter currency code (e.g., USD, EUR). If multiple currencies are mentioned, use the one directly associated with the deal value. If none, set to "Unknown".
- Merged entity: Only fill this for mergers. If the article says the merged company will be named, use that name. If it says the companies will operate under a new name, use that. Otherwise, null.

Example Input (acquisition): "Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining in $17 billion deal."
Example Output: {"parent_company":"Newmont","parent_company_ticker":null,"child_company":"Newcrest Mining Limited","child_company_ticker":null,"deal_amount":17,"deal_currency":"USD","article_type":"acquisition"}

Example Input (merger): "Rock Tech Lithium and Arcore to merge, forming NewCo."
Example Output: {"company_1":"Rock Tech Lithium","company_1_ticker":null,"company_2":"Arcore","company_2_ticker":null,"merged_entity":"NewCo","deal_amount":null,"deal_currency":"Unknown","article_type":"merger"}

If the article is about a failed or rumored deal, set article_type to "other" and leave other fields as null/Unknown.

2026/06/02 10:06:34 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 42: Proposed new text for merger_extractor: Your task is to extract key information about companies involved in a merger deal from a given article. You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:
    - If a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 10:08:03 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 43: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from articles about corporate transactions (e.g., acquisitions, mergers, etc.):

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If the article describes a merger of equals where there is no clear acquirer, list the first company mentioned as parent_company and the second as child_company. If no company names are present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). In a merger of equals, list the second company mentioned. If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article based on the transaction type. Common types: 'acquisition' (one company buys another), 'merger' (two companies combine, often with language like 'merge', 'merger of equals', 'combine'; no clear acquirer), 'joint_venture', 'divestiture', 'investment', etc. If the article is clearly a merger, do not default to 'acquisition'. Use 'acquisition' only when it is explicitly an acquisition. If no transaction type can be determined, use 'other'. Default to 'acquisition' only if the article describes a typical acquisition scenario and no other type applies.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- For mergers, if the article mention both companies but no clear acquirer, still populate parent_company and child_company as described, but set article_type to 'merger'. If the merger creates a new entity, you may list the original companies accordingly.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1 (Acquisition):
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],
  "child_company": "Azure Mining",
  "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"],
  "deal_amount": "4.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 2 (Acquisition with plain $):
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 3 (No currency indicator):
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "6.7 billion",
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 4 (Multiple numbers, acquisition):
Article: 'Newcrest Mining to buy Pretium Resources in a deal valued at C$3.5 billion. The combined entity expects $2.8 billion in revenue.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newcrest Mining",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Pretium Resources",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "3.5 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 5 (Merger):
Article: 'Glencore and Xstrata agree to merge in a $90 billion deal.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Glencore",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Xstrata",

  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "merger"
}

2026/06/02 10:09:21 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 46: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the news article about a corporate deal and classify it as Merger, Acquisition, or Other using the following detailed guidelines:

1. Merger: The deal results in a new combined entity, or two companies combine as equals. Key indicators:
   - A new company name or entity is explicitly mentioned.
   - Both companies' shareholders receive shares in the new company (often via stock swap).
   - Language like 'merger of equals,' 'combine,' 'consolidate,' or 'join forces' without one company clearly taking over.
   - No cash payment to one set of shareholders; instead, shares are exchanged.
   - Example: 'Company A and Company B announced a merger to form Company AB, with each owning 50% of the new entity.'

2. Acquisition: One company buys another, with the target becoming a subsidiary or being absorbed. Key indicators:
   - Terms like 'acquire,' 'buy,' 'purchase,' 'takeover,' or 'offer to acquire.'
   - Cash payment or a mix where one company's shareholders are bought out (even if part of the payment is in stock of the acquirer).
   - The acquirer remains the dominant entity; the target's brand may disappear or become a subsidiary.
   - Even if the headline calls it a 'merger,' if one company is paying cash for the other's shares, classify as Acquisition.
   - Example: 'Company A announced it will acquire Company B for $X per share in cash.'

3. Other: The article discusses potential, rumored, speculative, or failed deals, or deals that are not clearly mergers or acquisitions (e.g., joint ventures, partnerships, or unconfirmed talks). Key indicators:
   - Words like 'rumored,' 'potential,' 'would-be,' 'failed,' 'abandoned,' 'talks,' 'considering,' 'in discussions,' 'reportedly.'
   - No definitive agreement is announced, or the deal has been called off.
   - Example: 'Sources say Company A is in early talks to possibly merge with Company B.'

When classifying, read the entire article carefully, focusing on the deal's structure and language, not just the headline. If a deal is finalized and closed, it still falls under Merger or Acquisition based on the above.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. Extract a value ONLY if the article explicitly and unambiguously states the name of the resulting entity. This can happen in two ways: (1) The article announces a new, separate entity, e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'. (2) The article clearly states that one existing company will be the surviving entity after the merger, e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company' or 'Beta will be the surviving entity'. In both cases, the article must explicitly name the resulting company. If there is no explicit statement about the name of the merged entity (for example, the article only describes the deal but does not say which company will survive or if a new name is created), set this field to null. Do not infer or assume the merged entity based on the acquirer's name unless the article explicitly indicates it will be the resulting company. Be strict: only set if the article directly provides the merged entity's name.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company when extracting merged_entity.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

1. Merger:
   - Two companies combine to form a new, single entity.
   - Often described with phrases like: 'merger of equals', 'combine forces', 'join together', 'forming a new company'.
   - The resulting entity typically has a new name or a blend of the original names.
   - Example: 'Company A and Company B announced a merger to create Company C.'

2. Acquisition:
   - One company (the parent) buys or takes over another (the child).
   - Often described with terms like: 'acquire', 'purchase', 'takeover', 'buyout', 'absorb'.
   - The acquired company may cease to exist or operate as a subsidiary.
   - Example: 'Company X agreed to acquire Company Y for $Z billion.'

3. Important distinction: If the article explicitly states 'merger', treat as merger unless it clearly describes an acquisition structure (e.g., one company becoming a subsidiary). If it states 'acquisition', 'buy', or 'takeover', treat as acquisition. If both terms are used, rely on the official terminology of the deal (e.g., press release wording).

4. Other:
   - Classify as 'Other' if the deal is:
     - Potential, rumored, speculative, or under discussion but not finalized.
     - Failed, abandoned, or withdrawn.
     - A joint venture, partnership, or minority stake investment (not full merger/acquisition).
   - Look for words like 'exploring', 'considering', 'rumored', 'possible', 'terminated', 'talks collapsed', 'bid' (if hostile and not completed).

5. If the article's language is vague or does not clearly fall into Merger or Acquisition, default to 'Other'. Do not guess based on incomplete information.

You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:
    - If a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 10:14:58 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 60: Proposed new text for classifier: Classify the news article as one of three types: 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'. Follow the detailed criteria below.

**Merger**: Two or more independent companies combine to form a *new* entity. Key indicators:
- Explicit use of terms like 'merge', 'merger of equals', 'combine', 'join forces', 'consolidate'.
- The article names a *new combined company* (the 'merged entity').
- The deal is framed as a partnership or union, not a takeover.
- The resulting organization often has a new name or is described as a creation.

**Acquisition**: One company (the acquirer) purchases or takes over another (the target). Key indicators:
- Terms like 'acquire', 'buy', 'takeover', 'purchase', 'gain control of', 'snap up'.
- The target's brand or operations may be absorbed, but no new entity is created; the acquirer remains the dominant entity.
- The article mentions 'acquirer' and 'target', or shows one company buying the outstanding shares of another.
- Even if the acquired company keeps its name as a subsidiary, it's an acquisition if ownership changes.

**Other**: Use this category in any of these cases:
- The deal is explicitly 'rumored', 'potential', 'in talks', 'exploring', 'mulling', 'speculative', or labeled as 'buyout interest' without confirmation.
- The deal failed, was 'abandoned', 'rejected', 'withdrawn', or 'terminated'.
- The article merely discusses market speculation or analyst predictions without a firm announcement.
- The transaction does not clearly fit merger or acquisition (e.g., joint ventures, strategic alliances, spin-offs, divestitures, or asset sales).
- If you are unsure after applying the above criteria, default to 'Other'.

**Important Notes**:
- Focus on the *substantive* nature of the deal, not just the headline. Headlines can be misleading.
- A merger is often presented as a 'merger of equals', but even if one company is larger, it can still be a merger if a new legal entity is created.
- An acquisition can be friendly or hostile; the key is that one company absorbs another.
- For deals that are announced but not yet completed, classify them as merger or acquisition based on the announcement's language; only use 'Other' if there is explicit uncertainty like 'may', 'could', 'rumored', etc.

Output only the classification label: 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'.

2026/06/02 10:16:33 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 65: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' (default) if it describes an acquisition deal; otherwise specify the type.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],
  "child_company": "Azure Mining",
  "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"],
  "deal_amount": "4.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 2:
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 3:
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "6.7 billion",
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 4 (added clarity):
Article: 'Newcrest Mining to buy Pretium Resources in a deal valued at C$3.5 billion. The combined entity expects $2.8 billion in revenue.' (Note: multiple numbers)

  "parent_company": "Newcrest Mining",

2026/06/02 10:17:36 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 68: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the news article and classify it as a merger, acquisition, or other. Use these guidelines:
- Merger: Two companies combine to form a new entity. Look for phrases like 'merge', 'merger of equals', 'combine to form', or indications that both companies will cease to exist and a new company is created. Joint ventures or partnerships without new entity formation are not mergers.
- Acquisition: One company takes over another. The acquiring company remains, and the target is absorbed. Look for words like 'acquire', 'takeover', 'buy', 'purchase'. If a larger company buys a smaller one, it's typically an acquisition, even if called a merger for legal/tax reasons. Focus on who is the buyer and who is the target; if one company clearly dominates, classify as acquisition.
- Other: Use for potential, rumored, or failed deals, where the transaction is not final, has fallen through, or is merely speculative. Also use if the article discusses multiples deals or general M&A trends without a specific definitive deal.
- For ambiguous cases where companies of similar size merge but one is technically the acquirer, consider the language: if it emphasizes combination and a new name/entity, it's a merger; if it emphasizes one buying the other, it's an acquisition. If still uncertain after reading the full article, default to the category most supported by the language used.
- Do not extract any entities or amounts; this task is classification only.

2026/06/02 10:18:04 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 70: Proposed new text for merger_extractor: Your task is to extract key information about companies involved in a merger deal from a given article. You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. Extract a value ONLY if the article explicitly and unambiguously states the name of the resulting entity. This can happen in two ways: (1) The article announces a new, separate entity, e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'. (2) The article clearly states that one existing company will be the surviving entity after the merger, e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company' or 'Beta will be the surviving entity'. In both cases, the article must explicitly name the resulting company. If there is no explicit statement about the name of the merged entity (for example, the article only describes the deal but does not say which company will survive or if a new name is created), set this field to null. Do not infer or assume the merged entity based on the acquirer's name unless the article explicitly indicates it will be the resulting company. Be strict: only set if the article directly provides the merged entity's name.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company when extracting merged_entity.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 10:18:58 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 71: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each formatted as 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). Follow these rules:
    - If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding code: 'C$' → 'CAD', 'US$' → 'USD', 'A$' → 'AUD', etc.
    - If only a plain '$' (no letter prefix) is used and no other currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), default to 'USD'.
    - If the article uses a distinct non-dollar symbol (e.g., '£' for GBP), extract that code.
    - If no currency indicator is present at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' (default) if it describes an acquisition deal; otherwise specify the type.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must always include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use exchange abbreviations exactly as provided.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Normalize the text by removing modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' becomes '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B' or 'bn', and 'million' not 'M'. For integer amounts, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- If a field is absent, output None.

Examples:

Example 1:

Example 2:
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {"parent_company": "Newmont", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "17.8 billion", "deal_currency": "USD", "article_type": "acquisition"}

Example 3:
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output: {"parent_company": "Rio Tinto", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "6.7 billion", "deal_currency": "Unknown", "article_type": "acquisition"}

Example 4:
Article: 'Newcrest Mining to buy Pretium Resources in a deal valued at C$3.5 billion. The combined entity expects $2.8 billion in revenue.' (Multiple numbers)
Output: {"parent_company": "Newcrest Mining", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Pretium Resources", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "3.5 billion", "deal_currency": "CAD", "article_type": "acquisition"}

Example 5 (no deal amount or tickers):

2026/06/02 10:20:22 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 73: Proposed new text for classifier: Classify the news article deal type as 'Merger', 'Acquisition', or 'Other'.

- Merger: Two companies combine to form a new entity. Both original companies cease to exist or their operations are integrated under a new name/entity. Look for language like 'merge', 'combination', 'merger of equals', 'join forces to create', or 'new entity'. Even if shareholders of one company receive stock in the new entity, if the deal is structured as a true consolidation and neither side is clearly acquiring the other, label it Merger.

- Acquisition: One company (the acquirer) takes over another (the target). The target may become a subsidiary or be absorbed entirely. The acquirer retains control. Key terms: 'acquire', 'takeover', 'purchase', 'buyout', 'tender offer', 'absorb', or 'bought'. If the article states that Company A will buy Company B and B's shareholders will receive cash or A's stock, and B ceases to exist independently, it's an Acquisition.

- Other: Use when the article discusses potential, rumored, or speculative deals; failed or terminated transactions; joint ventures; minority stake purchases; or any scenario where the classification does not clearly fit Merger or Acquisition. Also use if the article primarily describes a possible future deal without confirmation.

Important: Base the classification on the deal structure and language used. If the article explicitly calls it a 'merger' but describes a takeover, use the economic substance. If both companies combine to form a new entity and neither is dominant, it's a merger. If one company clearly buys the other, it's an acquisition. In case of ambiguity, default to Other.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.

- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:
    - If a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.
