- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article based on the transaction described. Use 'acquisition' if one company is buying another (e.g., keywords: 'acquires', 'acquisition', 'purchase', 'takeover'). Use 'merger' if two companies combine to form a new entity or the language indicates a merger (e.g., 'merger', 'merge', 'all-stock merger', 'merger of equals', 'combined company'). If the article does not clearly indicate a deal type but describes an acquisition-like event, default to 'acquisition'. For other types (e.g., joint venture, partnership), specify the type explicitly.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.

2026/06/02 10:23:23 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 81: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the news article and classify the deal as 'Merger', 'Acquisition', or 'Other', based on the following detailed criteria:

**Merger:**
- Two or more companies combine to form a completely new entity. Both original companies cease to exist, and a new company is created, often with a new name.
- The companies involved are typically of similar size and market power, and the transaction is often described as a 'merger of equals'.
- Control is shared; no single company dominates the new entity.
- Look for phrases like 'merge to form', 'combination creating a new company', 'all-stock merger of equals'.
- The deal is completed or in progress (not just rumored or potential).

**Acquisition:**
- One company (the acquirer) takes over another company (the target). The acquirer remains in existence, while the target may be absorbed, become a subsidiary, or lose its original name.
- The acquirer is usually larger or more dominant financially. The transaction often involves a cash payment, stock swap where the acquirer's shareholders end up with majority control, or a buyout.
- Look for phrases like 'acquires', 'buys', 'takeover', 'purchase of'.
- Even if the article uses the word 'merger', if the structure is that one company is clearly acquiring the other (e.g., one disappears, or one set of shareholders gets less than 50% of the combined entity), classify it as an acquisition.

**Other:**
- Any deal that is potential, rumored, speculative, rejected, failed, or withdrawn, regardless of whether it would have been a merger or acquisition if completed.
- Articles that discuss past deals in a historical context without a current transaction.
- News about regulatory approvals for a previously reported deal (unless it confirms the deal type, but the deal itself is still the subject).
Note: If the article only mentions that a deal might happen or is being considered, classify as 'Other'.

**Procedure:**
1. Read the article carefully. Identify the key parties and the nature of the transaction.
2. Determine the deal status: if it's not a definite, completed, or ongoing deal, it's 'Other'.
3. If the deal is firm, assess whether a new entity is formed with shared control (Merger) or one company takes over another (Acquisition).
4. Use the linguistic cues and structural details to make the final classification.

2026/06/02 10:23:58 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 84: Proposed new text for merger_extractor: Your task is to extract key information about companies involved in a merger deal from a given article. You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:
    - If a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 10:25:43 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 86: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the deal type. Use 'merger' if the article explicitly describes a merger where two companies combine to form a new entity (e.g., 'merge', 'merger of equals'). Use 'acquisition' if one company acquires another (e.g., 'acquires', 'buys', 'purchase'). If the article does not describe an acquisition or merger, specify the appropriate type (e.g., 'joint venture', 'divestiture'). Default to 'acquisition' if uncertain.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],
  "child_company": "Azure Mining",
  "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"],
  "deal_amount": "4.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 2:
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 3:
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "6.7 billion",
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 4:
Article: 'Newcrest Mining to buy Pretium Resources in a deal valued at C$3.5 billion. The combined entity expects $2.8 billion in revenue.' (Note: multiple numbers)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newcrest Mining",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Pretium Resources",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "3.5 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 5 (merger):
Article: 'ABC Corp and XYZ Inc announce merger to form Combined Holdings. The transaction is valued at $5 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "ABC Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "XYZ Inc",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "5 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "merger"
}

Follow these guidelines precisely.

   - 'merger': Two companies combine to form a new entity.

   - 'company_2': The second merging company.

   - 'merged_entity': The name of the new entity formed after the merger, ONLY if the article explicitly states a new name. 5. 'deal_amount': The deal value as a string with unit if possible (e.g., '5 billion'). If not mentioned, null.
6. 'deal_currency': The currency of the deal amount (e.g., 'USD', 'AUD'). If not clear, use 'Unknown'.

Provide the output as a JSON object with the above fields. If a field is not extractable, set it to null.

You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:
    - If a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 10:29:29 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 95: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the type of deal described. Use 'acquisition' if the article describes an acquisition (e.g., one company buying another, a takeover, a buyout). Use 'merger' if it describes a merger (e.g., two companies combining to form a new entity, often using terms like 'merge', 'merger of equals', or 'business combination'). If the article describes another type of transaction (e.g., joint venture, spin-off, divestiture, investment), specify that type. If it is unquestionably an acquisition but no other type fits, default to 'acquisition'.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- Distinguish between acquisition and merger: Look for explicit language. If the article uses 'acquire', 'buy', 'takeover', it's likely an acquisition. If it uses 'merge', 'combination', 'merge of equals', it's a merger. In mergers, often both companies are equal, and there may be no clear parent/child, but still extract if possible.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

2026/06/02 10:30:28 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 96: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the given news article and perform the following tasks: 

1. Identify the two main companies involved in the deal. Extract their names (company_1 and company_2) and, if available, their stock tickers (company_1_ticker, company_2_ticker). 
2. Determine if a new merged entity is created. If so, extract its name (merged_entity); otherwise, set it to None. 
3. Extract the deal amount and its currency (deal_amount, deal_currency). 
4. Classify the article into one of three types: 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'. 

Classification guidelines: 
- Merger: The article describes two companies combining to form an entirely new entity. Look for language like 'merge', 'combination', 'new company', 'joint entity', or explicit naming of a new organization. If there is no clear new entity formed, it is NOT a merger. 
- Acquisition: One company buys or takes over another. Look for terms like 'acquire', 'takeover', 'buy', 'purchase', or 'deal' where one party is clearly the buyer and the other the target. The target company typically ceases to exist as an independent entity, even if its brand is retained. 
- Other: Use this category for articles about potential, rumored, speculative, failed, or incomplete deals. Also include any deal that does not fit the above definitions (e.g., partnerships, strategic alliances, investments without change of control). 

Extraction rules: 
- Company names: Extract the full legal or commonly used name. If multiple names are mentioned, choose the ones most directly involved. Avoid using abbreviations unless they are the primary way the entity is referenced. 
- Tickers: Provide uppercase stock symbols if explicitly mentioned (e.g., 'NYSE: ABC'). If not available, set to None. 
- Merged entity: Only fill if a new company name is explicitly stated as a result of the merger. If the article says 'will combine to form XYZ', then merged_entity = 'XYZ'. If one company is simply absorbed, set to None. 
- Deal amount and currency: Extract the numerical value and ISO currency code (e.g., 'USD', 'EUR'). If the amount is a range, use the lower bound. If not monetary (e.g., stock swap), attempt to infer or set to None. 

Examples: 
1. Article: 'Company A to acquire Company B for $5 billion.' -> company_1: Company A (buyer), company_2: Company B (target), merged_entity: None, deal_amount: 5 billion, deal_currency: USD, article_type: acquisition. 
2. Article: 'Company X and Company Y announce merger to form NewCo.' -> company_1: Company X, company_2: Company Y, merged_entity: NewCo, deal_amount: None, article_type: merger. 
3. Article: 'Talks of a potential merger between Firm P and Firm Q fall apart.' -> article_type: Other (failed deal). 

Output the results in a structured JSON with the fields: company_1, company_1_ticker, company_2, company_2_ticker, merged_entity, deal_amount, deal_currency, article_type.

2026/06/02 10:31:39 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 102: Proposed new text for merger_extractor: Your task is to extract key information about companies involved in a merger deal from a given article. You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. Extract a value ONLY if the article explicitly and unambiguously states the name of the resulting entity. This can happen in two ways: (1) The article announces a new, separate entity, e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'. (2) The article clearly states that one existing company will be the surviving entity after the merger, e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company' or 'Beta will be the surviving entity'. In both cases, the article must explicitly name the resulting company. If there is no explicit statement about the name of the merged entity (for example, the article only describes the deal but does not say which company will survive or if a new name is created), set this field to null. Do not infer or assume the merged entity based on the acquirer's name unless the article explicitly indicates it will be the resulting company. Be strict: only set if the article directly provides the merged entity's name.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company when extracting merged_entity.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 10:32:43 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 103: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the deal type based on article content. Use 'acquisition' if one company buys another, typically indicated by words like 'acquire', 'buy', 'takeover', 'purchase'. Use 'merger' if the article describes a combination of two companies, often using terms like 'merger', 'all-stock merger', 'merge', 'combine', 'merger of equals'. If neither, specify the type. Default to 'acquisition' only if it clearly is an acquisition, otherwise choose the most appropriate label.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1 (Acquisition):
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'

  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],

  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",

  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 3 (No currency specified):

  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,

  "article_type": "acquisition"

Example 4 (Multiple numbers):

First, determine the `article_type`:
- If the article describes two companies combining to form a new entity, classify as "merger".
- If one company is taking over another, classify as "acquisition".
- If the deal is only potential, rumored, or has failed, classify as "other".

Then, extract the following fields based on the `article_type`:

For `acquisition`:
- `parent_company` (string): The name of the acquiring company.
- `parent_company_ticker` (string or null): Stock ticker of the acquiring company, if mentioned.
- `child_company` (string): The name of the company being acquired.
- `child_company_ticker` (string or null): Stock ticker of the target company, if mentioned.

For `merger`:
- `company_1` (string): Name of the first merging company.
- `company_1_ticker` (string or null): Its ticker, if available.
- `company_2` (string): Name of the second merging company.
- `company_2_ticker` (string or null): Its ticker, if available.
- `merged_entity` (string or null): The name of the new combined entity if explicitly stated. If no new name is given (e.g., one company keeps its name), set to null.

For `other` (potential/rumored/failed deals):
- `companies` (list of objects): Each object should have `name` and `ticker` (or null) for companies mentioned in connection with the deal.

Additionally, for all types, include:
- `deal_amount` (string or null): The total value of the deal as stated in the article (e.g., "3.5 billion"), or null if not mentioned.
- `deal_currency` (string or null): The three-letter currency code (e.g., "USD", "CAD") if determinable, else null.
- `summary` (string): A concise 1-2 sentence summary of the deal.

All extracted company names should be exactly as they appear in the article. If a field is not applicable or not found, set its value to null. Ensure the output is valid JSON.

2026/06/02 10:35:36 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 113: Proposed new text for merger_extractor: Your task is to extract key information about companies involved in a merger deal from a given article. You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. Extract a value ONLY if the article explicitly and unambiguously states the name of the resulting entity. This can happen in two ways: (1) The article announces a new, separate entity, e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'. (2) The article clearly states that one existing company will be the surviving entity after the merger, e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company' or 'Beta will be the surviving entity'. In both cases, the article must explicitly name the resulting company. If there is no explicit statement about the name of the merged entity (for example, the article only describes the deal but does not say which company will survive or if a new name is created), set this field to null. Do not infer or assume the merged entity based on the acquirer's name unless the article explicitly indicates it will be the resulting company. Be strict: only set if the article directly provides the merged entity's name.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company when extracting merged_entity.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 10:36:54 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 115: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article's transaction type. Use 'acquisition' if one company is clearly buying another (the acquirer is identifiable). Use 'merger' if two companies combine to form a new entity, often described with terms like 'merger of equals', 'all-stock merger', or the companies' names are joined. Use other labels such as 'joint_venture', 'divestiture', etc., if applicable. Default to 'acquisition' only when the deal is a buyout but the distinction is ambiguous.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Your task is to analyze the given article and output a JSON object with the following fields:

1. **article_type**: Classify the deal as one of:
   - `merger`: Two companies combine to form a new entity or merge operations.
   - `acquisition`: One company takes over another (the target becomes a subsidiary or is absorbed).
   - `Other`: The article discusses a potential, rumored, failed, or denied deal, or it is not strictly about a merger/acquisition.

2. For **acquisition** articles, extract:
   - `parent_company`: The acquiring company's name.
   - `parent_company_ticker`: Stock ticker(s) of the acquirer, in the format "EXCHANGE:TICKER" (e.g., "ASX:PLS"). If multiple, list them. If none mentioned, use `null`.
   - `child_company`: The target company's name.
   - `child_company_ticker`: Ticker(s) of the target, same format. If none, `null`.
   - `deal_amount`: The numeric value of the deal as a string, e.g., "3.5 billion". Exclude the currency; if not mentioned, `null`.
   - `deal_currency`: The three-letter currency code (e.g., "USD", "CAD"). If not specified, use "Unknown".

3. For **merger** articles, extract:
   - `company_1` and `company_2`: The names of the two merging companies. Order does not matter.
   - `company_1_ticker` and `company_2_ticker`: Their tickers, same format as above.
   - `merged_entity`: The name of the newly formed entity, if explicitly mentioned. Otherwise, `null`.
   - `deal_amount` and `deal_currency`: As defined above.

4. For **Other** articles, only `article_type` is required; all other fields can be omitted or set to `null`.

**Important guidelines**:
- Extract information only from the article text. Do not infer or use external knowledge.
- If multiple deals are mentioned, focus on the main deal or the one most prominently described.
- For tickers, capture them exactly as written (e.g., "NYSE:TWTR"). If the article provides only the symbol without exchange, you may attempt to infer the exchange from context, but only if confident; otherwise use the symbol alone or `null`.
- For `deal_amount`, normalize the number and scale (e.g., "3,500,000,000" becomes "3.5 billion"). If the amount is a range, pick the most definitive value (e.g., the final agreed price).
- If a field cannot be determined, set it to `null` for tickers/amount/merged_entity, or "Unknown" for `deal_currency`.

You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. If the article explicitly states that a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name. If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company. If neither a new entity nor a surviving company is specified, set this field to null. Do not assume the acquirer is always the merged entity unless the article indicates it will be the resulting company.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company when extracting merged_entity.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

Given a news article about a corporate transaction, extract structured information in JSON format. The output must include the following fields, adapting the schema based on the deal type:

1. **article_type**: String, one of 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'other'.
   - Use 'merger' when two or more companies combine to form a new entity or one of the existing entities becomes the surviving combined company, and the deal is confirmed (not rumored, potential, or failed).
   - Use 'acquisition' when one company buys or takes over another company, regardless of whether the target continues to operate separately, and the deal is confirmed.
   - Use 'other' for any deal that is described as rumored, potential, proposed, failed, abandoned, or not yet confirmed. Also use 'other' if the article does not provide enough clarity to classify it as a definitive merger or acquisition.

2. For **merger** articles, extract:
   - `company_1`: String, the name of the first company involved. If multiple, pick the first clearly mentioned.
   - `company_1_ticker`: String or null, the stock ticker symbol of company_1 if provided, else null.
   - `company_2`: String, the name of the second company involved. If more than two, list the main two parties.
   - `company_2_ticker`: String or null, the stock ticker of company_2 if provided, else null.
   - `merged_entity`: String or null. If the article explicitly states that a new company will be formed and gives it a name (e.g., 'NewCo'), extract that name. If the merger will result in one of the existing companies surviving as the combined entity, but no new name is created, set this to null. Do not use a company name as the merged entity unless it is a newly created name.
   - `deal_amount`: String or null, the total value of the deal as mentioned (e.g., '3.4 billion', '500 million'), or null if not mentioned. Keep the amount in the original wording with units.
   - `deal_currency`: String, the currency of the deal amount (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'), or 'Unknown' if no currency is specified.

3. For **acquisition** articles, extract:
   - `parent_company`: String, the name of the acquiring company (the buyer).
   - `parent_company_ticker`: String or null, its ticker symbol if provided, else null.
   - `child_company`: String, the name of the company being acquired (the target).
   - `child_company_ticker`: String or null, its ticker symbol if provided, else null.
   - `deal_amount`: String or null, the deal value as a string (e.g., '2.4 billion'), or null if not mentioned.
   - `deal_currency`: String, the currency code or 'Unknown'.
   Note: If the acquisition involves multiple targets, extract the primary one mentioned.

4. For **other** deals (rumored, potential, failed, etc.), still attempt to extract the entities and deal amount as if it were a merger or acquisition, based on the language used, but set `article_type` to 'other'. For example, if the article talks about a potential acquisition, use the acquisition schema; if a potential merger, use the merger schema. If the nature is ambiguous, default to the acquisition schema (parent/child). If no entities can be determined, set all names to null. Deal amount and currency may also be null if not provided.

5. General rules:
   - For deal amount, preserve the exact phrasing from the text (e.g., '$3.4 billion' becomes '3.4 billion' with the currency extracted separately). Do not convert units unless necessary for consistency, but if you do, maintain the order of magnitude.
   - For tickers, if the exchange symbol is provided in parentheses or brackets after a company name, extract it. If no ticker is given, set to null.
   - Ensure that all extracted strings are stripped of extra whitespace.
   - If multiple deal values are mentioned (e.g., enterprise value and equity value), extract the one that seems to represent the total deal consideration. If unclear, extract the most prominent one.
   - If currency is mentioned in the deal amount phrase (e.g., 'CAD 500 million'), set deal_currency to the appropriate code; otherwise, if only a symbol like '$' is used, infer the most likely currency based on context (e.g., country of the companies) or set to 'Unknown' if ambiguous.

The final output must be a valid JSON object with exactly these fields for the identified article type. No additional commentary.

2026/06/02 10:42:26 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 127: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' if it describes one company purchasing another (e.g., 'acquires', 'to buy', 'acquisition', 'purchase'). Classify as 'merger' if it describes a merger of equals or combination of two companies into a new entity (e.g., 'merger', 'merge', 'combination', 'merge of equals'). If the article type cannot be determined, default to 'acquisition' ONLY if there is a clear acquiring and target company. If the deal is explicitly another type (e.g., 'joint venture', 'spin-off'), output that type. In general, prefer 'merger' when the article explicitly states a merger, not an acquisition.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'

2026/06/02 10:44:05 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 130: Proposed new text for classifier: Task: Analyze the given news article and classify it as either 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'. Use the definitions and examples below to guide your decision.

Definitions:
- Merger: Two companies combine to form a new entity, or one company absorbs the other with the surviving company being considered the merged entity. This may include strategic mergers where the combined company operates under one of the original names. The key is that the deal is structured as a merger, with both companies ceasing to exist in their original forms (or one ceases) and a new or surviving entity emerges. Look for terms like 'merger', 'combine', 'consolidation'.
- Acquisition: One company (the parent or acquirer) takes over another company (the target or child), and the target ceases to exist as an independent entity. The acquirer typically pays cash, stock, or a combination. Look for terms like 'acquisition', 'takeover', 'buyout', 'purchase'.
- Other: Any deal that is not a finalized merger or acquisition. This includes potential, rumored, or failed deals; discussions; regulatory approvals pending; withdrawn offers; or other non-deal news. If the article is primarily about speculation or a deal that did not complete, classify as 'Other'. If the article is not about a specific deal, also 'Other'.

Examples based on feedback:

Example 1 (Merger):
Article snippet: "Karora Resources and Westgold announced an agreement to merge, creating a new leading gold mining company. The combined entity will operate under the Westgold name, with the deal valued at $3.4 billion USD."
Classification: merger
Reason: Two companies are combining, and although the new entity uses the Westgold name, it is described as a merger forming a new company. The deal is not just an acquisition because both sets of shareholders will own the combined entity, not just one buying the other outright.

Example 2 (Acquisition):
Article snippet: "Newmont Corporation has completed the acquisition of Newcrest Mining Limited for approximately $17 billion. Newmont will now incorporate Newcrest's assets into its global operations."
Classification: acquisition
Reason: One company (Newmont) is taking over another (Newcrest), with Newcrest ceasing to exist. Clear language of acquisition.

Example 3 (Acquisition):
Article snippet: "Pilbara Minerals has made a takeover offer for Latin Resources, valuing the lithium developer at hundreds of millions of dollars. The deal will see Pilbara acquire all shares of Latin Resources."
Classification: acquisition
Reason: Pilbara Minerals is acquiring Latin Resources, a typical acquisition. The language of 'takeover offer' and 'acquire all shares' indicates acquisition.

Example 4 (Other):
Article snippet: "Reports suggest that Company X is in early-stage talks with Company Y for a potential merger, but no formal agreement has been reached."
Classification: Other
Reason: The deal is only rumored/potential, not finalized. Such speculative or preliminary news should be classified as Other.

If the article does not discuss any deal, or the deal status is unclear, classify as Other.

Output format: Return only one word: 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'.

2026/06/02 10:45:54 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 135: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert in extracting structured data from acquisition deal articles. Output a JSON object with the following fields:

- parent_company: Acquiring company name (string or null).
- parent_company_ticker: List of strings in format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL', e.g., 'TSX:TCP'. Include all mentioned exchanges; otherwise null.
- child_company: Target/acquired company name (string or null).
- child_company_ticker: List of strings, same format, for the target company; otherwise null.
- deal_amount: Transaction value as string with number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Exclude currency symbols, abbreviations like 'bn', and modifiers like 'approximately'. Use 'billion' and 'million' (not 'B', 'M'). Integer values: '5 billion' not '5.0 billion'. If not present, output null.
- deal_currency: Uppercase currency code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). Determine from explicit symbol or code:
  - 'C$' → 'CAD', 'US$' → 'USD', 'A$' → 'AUD', etc.
  - Plain '$' (no letter prefix) without explicit currency mention → 'USD'.
  - If no symbol or code at all → 'Unknown'.
  - Do not infer from company country.
- article_type: 'acquisition' if describing an acquisition; otherwise describe the type.

Extraction rules:
- Ticker symbols must include exchange prefix. Use abbreviations as given in the article.
- For deal_amount: Only extract the specific transaction value. Ignore other numbers (revenues, market caps, etc.). Look for phrases like 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price'. Normalize by removing adverbs ('roughly', 'about').
- If any field is missing, output null.

Examples:

Example 1:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {"parent_company": "Titanium Corp", "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"], "child_company": "Azure Mining", "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"], "deal_amount": "4.2 billion", "deal_currency": "CAD", "article_type": "acquisition"}

Example 2:
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {"parent_company": "Newmont", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "17.8 billion", "deal_currency": "USD", "article_type": "acquisition"}

2026/06/02 10:47:21 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 138: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the given news article and extract structured information about a merger, acquisition, or similar deal. Follow these steps:

1. **Classify the deal type** as one of:
   - `merger`: Two or more companies combine to form a new entity.
   - `acquisition`: One company takes over another (the target becomes a subsidiary or is absorbed).
   - `Other`: The article discusses a potential, rumored, failed, or otherwise non-completed deal, or does not involve an actual merger/acquisition.

2. **Extract company details based on deal type**:
   - For a **merger**:
     - `company_1`: Name of the first merging company.
     - `company_1_ticker`: Its stock ticker symbol (if publicly traded and mentioned), else `null`.
     - `company_2`: Name of the second merging company.
     - `company_2_ticker`: Its stock ticker symbol (if mentioned), else `null`.
     - `merged_entity`: The name of the newly formed company, if explicitly stated. If no new name is given or the companies continue under their existing names, set to `null`.
   - For an **acquisition**:
     - `parent_company`: Name of the acquiring company (the buyer).
     - `parent_company_ticker`: Its stock ticker symbol (if mentioned), else `null`.
     - `child_company`: Name of the company being acquired (the target).
     - `child_company_ticker`: Its stock ticker symbol (if mentioned), else `null`.
   - For type **Other**: All company fields should be `null` unless a clear acquiring/target or merging pair is identifiable, but if the deal is not completed, set `article_type` to `Other` and leave company fields as `null` unless you are confident about the parties.

3. **Extract financial details**:
   - `deal_amount`: The total value of the deal as a number (e.g., `3400000000`). If the article mentions multiple figures (e.g., enterprise value, equity value), choose the most commonly reported headline number. If no amount is given, output `null`.
   - `deal_currency`: The ISO 4217 currency code of the deal amount (e.g., `USD`, `EUR`). If amount is `null`, set currency to `null`.

4. **Output format**: Return a JSON object with exactly the following keys:
   - `article_type` (string: "merger", "acquisition", or "Other")
   - `company_1` (string or null)
   - `company_1_ticker` (string or null)
   - `company_2` (string or null)
   - `company_2_ticker` (string or null)
   - `merged_entity` (string or null)
   - `parent_company` (string or null)
   - `parent_company_ticker` (string or null)
   - `child_company` (string or null)
   - `child_company_ticker` (string or null)
   - `deal_amount` (number or null)
   - `deal_currency` (string or null)

5. **Important rules**:
   - If a deal involves multiple parties (e.g., a consortium), choose the two primary entities or the main buyer/target. If unclear, set relevant fields to `null`.
   - For mergers, `merged_entity` is distinct from `company_1` and `company_2`; do not reuse a merging company's name as the merged entity unless the article explicitly states that the new entity will bear that name.
   - If the article mentions a deal but it is only a rumor, speculation, or has been terminated, set `article_type` to "Other" and all other fields to `null` except you may fill in companies if they are unambiguously identified, but prioritize accuracy.
   - Extract ticker symbols exactly as they appear (e.g., "RIO" for Rio Tinto). If not available, use `null`.
   - Deal amount and currency: If the article states a range, use the midpoint or the most definitive figure. If multiple currencies are mentioned, use the primary currency of the deal.
   - Ensure all string values are properly trimmed and exactly as in the article (no extra commentary).

Fields to Extract:
- parent_company: Acquiring company's name (string). If absent, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: List of strings, each formatted as 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: Target/acquired company's name (string). If absent, use None.
- child_company_ticker: List of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: Transaction value as a string with number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: Full uppercase currency code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). Derive from explicit indicators: 'C$' → 'CAD', 'US$' → 'USD', 'A$' → 'AUD', etc. For plain '$' with no other currency info, default to 'USD'. If no indicator at all, use 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Classify as 'acquisition' (default) if it describes an acquisition deal; otherwise specify the actual type.

Rules:
1. Ticker symbols: Must include exchange prefix (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ') exactly as in the article.
2. Deal amount: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues or market caps. Look for phrases: 'worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', etc. Normalize by removing modifiers like 'approximately', but keep the exact number-word pairing. Use 'billion' and 'million' (e.g., '17.8 billion'). For integers, write '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
3. Currency: Follow explicit indicators only. Do not infer from company country. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract accordingly.
4. If any field is truly absent, output None.

Examples:
Example 1:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'

Example 2:
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {"parent_company": "Newmont", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "17.8 billion", "deal_currency": "USD", "article_type": "acquisition"}

Example 3:
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)

2026/06/02 10:49:43 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 144: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the news article and classify the deal type as 'Merger', 'Acquisition', or 'Other'. 

- Merger: Two companies combine to form a new entity, often through mutual agreement and typically of comparable size. The result is a single new organization. Look for phrases like 'merge with', 'combination', 'new company', 'joint'.
- Acquisition: One company (the acquirer) takes over another (the target). The target becomes a subsidiary or is absorbed. The acquirer usually buys a majority stake or all outstanding shares. Look for terms like 'acquire', 'buy', 'take over', 'purchase', 'bid'.
- Other: Any deal that is not finalized or is speculative. This includes rumored, potential, proposed, failed, or withdrawn deals, regardless of structure. If the article mentions ongoing talks, possible interest, or that the deal may not proceed, classify as Other.

When determining the type, consider the overall narrative: if the article states that Company A and Company B will merge to create a new company, it is a Merger. If Company A will buy Company B and integrate it, it is an Acquisition. Do not rely solely on the presence of specific words; ensure the context reflects the deal's nature.

2026/06/02 10:50:50 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 147: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' (default) if it describes an acquisition deal; otherwise specify the type.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],
  "child_company": "Azure Mining",
  "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"],
  "deal_amount": "4.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 2:
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 3:
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "6.7 billion",
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 4:
Article: 'Newcrest Mining to buy Pretium Resources in a deal valued at C$3.5 billion. The combined entity expects $2.8 billion in revenue.' (Note: multiple numbers)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newcrest Mining",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,

2026/06/02 10:53:11 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 151: Proposed new text for classifier: Objective: Extract structured information from a news article describing a business deal (merger or acquisition). If the article does not describe a completed merger or acquisition but mentions a potential, rumored, or failed deal, or if the article is not primarily about a deal, classify the article_type as 'Other'.

For any field not found, output null (i.e., None).

Fields to extract:
1. company_1: The name of the first company involved. If multiple companies, select the two primary participants; company_1 should be the one mentioned first or the acquirer in an acquisition.
2. company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol of company_1, if provided in the article (e.g., in parentheses after the company name). Otherwise null.
3. company_2: The name of the second company involved.
4. company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol of company_2, if provided. Otherwise null.
5. merged_entity: After a merger, if a new company name is explicitly stated as the resulting entity, provide that name. For acquisitions, this field is always null. If a merger occurs but no new entity name is given (e.g., the merged company retains one of the original names), output null. Only output a non-null value when a distinct new name is clearly indicated in the article.
6. deal_amount: The total value of the deal as a number with units (e.g., '2.8 billion', '3.4 billion'). Do not include the currency symbol or code in this field; keep the numeric scale (billion, million, etc.).
7. deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount, expressed as the ISO 4217 alphabetic code (e.g., 'USD', 'AUD'). If not mentioned, output null.
8. article_type: Classify as one of 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'.
   - 'merger': Use when the article describes two or more companies combining to form a new entity or explicitly uses terms like 'merge', 'merger', 'combination', and the deal is not a straightforward purchase. Typically, both companies agree to unite, and often a new company name is announced.
   - 'acquisition': Use when one company purchases or takes over another. Look for terms like 'acquire', 'buy', 'takeover', 'purchase', and the acquired company becomes part of the acquirer or its subsidiary.
   - 'Other': Use for potential, rumored, or failed deals, or if the article does not clearly indicate a completed merger or acquisition. Also use if the article is not primarily about a merger/acquisition (e.g., a profile, analysis, or unrelated news).

Detailed Guidelines:
- Read the entire article carefully to understand the nature of the deal and extract all requested details.
- Pay close attention to language indicating the deal type: phrases like 'agreed to merge' or 'announced a merger' strongly suggest 'merger'; phrases like 'acquired', 'bought', or 'takeover' suggest 'acquisition'. If both terms appear, consider the overall context and the fate of the companies.
- For merged_entity: Only set it if a new legal entity name is explicitly given. For example, if company A and company B merge to form 'ABC Corp', set merged_entity to 'ABC Corp'. If they merge and continue under one of their existing names, set merged_entity to null.
- If the article mentions multiple deals or companies, identify the primary deal discussed. If no single deal is clear, set all fields to null and article_type to 'Other'.
- Ensure the deal_amount and deal_currency are extracted accurately. If the deal value is expressed in a different scale (e.g., millions), include that in the amount string.
- If a field is truly not present, output null; do not invent values.

Examples of correct extraction:
- For a merger where Phoenix Resources and Quantum Mining combine and the article states they will form 'Phoenix Resources', company_1='Phoenix Resources', company_2='Quantum Mining', merged_entity='Phoenix Resources' (if it's the chosen name for the new entity; otherwise null), article_type='merger'.
- For a merger where Karora Resources and Westgold merge but no new name is given, company_1='Karora Resources', company_2='Westgold', merged_entity=null, article_type='merger'.
- For an acquisition where one company buys another, company_1 is the acquirer, company_2 is the target, merged_entity=null, article_type='acquisition'.
- For a potential deal that is not finalized, article_type='Other' and all fields may be null if no details are certain.

You must output a JSON object with the following fields. Adhere strictly to the extraction rules for each field.

Fields:
- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. Do not add suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd', unless the article explicitly and consistently includes them as part of the company's name in that context. If multiple names are used, choose the one that is the subject of the merger (e.g., the company being acquired or the acquirer). Typically, the two main companies mentioned in the merger are company_1 and company_2.
- company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:
    - If a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.
