2026/06/02 10:57:59 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 162: Proposed new text for classifier: Your task is to read a news article and classify the deal type as 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'other'. Follow these detailed guidelines:

Definitions:
- Merger: Two or more companies combine to form a new entity. The original companies cease to exist in their previous form. Often described as 'merger of equals', 'combination', or 'consolidation'. Look for explicit mention of a new entity being created.
- Acquisition: One company (the acquirer) purchases a controlling stake or all assets of another company (the target). The target may be absorbed or become a subsidiary. Described as 'acquire', 'takeover', 'buyout', 'purchase', 'deal to buy'.
- Other: Use this for deals that are not yet definitive (potential, rumored, exploratory, or failed). Even if the article describes a merger or acquisition structure, if it's stated as a possibility, rumor, or if the deal has fallen through, classify as 'Other'. Also use 'Other' if the article is not about a specific deal (e.g., general industry analysis) or if the deal type is unclear.

Important Distinctions:
- If the article says companies are 'merging' or 'forming a new joint entity', it's a merger.
- If the article says one company is 'acquiring' another, it's an acquisition.
- Be cautious: Sometimes the term 'merge' is used loosely. Focus on the structure: Is a new entity created? If yes, merger. If one company survives and the other is absorbed, acquisition.
- For stock-for-stock transactions, the classification depends on the resulting ownership and entity. If both companies' shareholders receive shares in a new holding company, it's a merger. If one company's shareholders end up controlling the combined entity and the other company is dissolved, it's likely an acquisition.
- Existence of a new name does not automatically make it a merger. An acquisition can also result in rebranding.

Edge Cases:
- When two companies combine but one is clearly the dominant party (e.g., 'X acquires Y and they will operate under X's name'), it's an acquisition, even if the article says 'merge'.
- If the article uses phrases like 'all-stock merger' but the deal is structured as one company issuing shares to buy the other, check who ends up in control. If it's a true merger of equals, classify as merger; otherwise acquisition.
- If the deal is called a 'merger' but is actually a takeover (e.g., hostile), classify as acquisition.
- For joint ventures, they are not mergers or acquisitions; classify as 'Other' if it's not a full combination.

Process:
1. Read the article headline and first paragraphs to identify the deal type.
2. Determine if the deal is completed, announced as definitive, or is speculative. If the latter, it's 'Other'.
3. Look for language indicating structure: 'merge to form', 'acquire', 'buy', 'takeover', 'combination', 'new entity', etc.
4. If deal is definitive, classify as merger or acquisition based on the structure described. If the article is ambiguous, consider industry norms.
5. Default to 'Other' if the article does not describe a specific, completed or agreed-upon deal.

Remember: The article_type field you output must be exactly one of: 'merger', 'acquisition', 'other'.

- company_1: The name of the first company involved in the merger. Extract the name as it appears in the article, using the primary or most common reference. - company_1_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_1, if mentioned in the article. The ticker is often enclosed in parentheses after the company name, like 'Company Name (TICKER)'. If no ticker is provided, set this field to null.
- company_2: The name of the second company involved in the merger. Similar rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:
    - If a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

- Classify as 'other' if the article discusses a potential, rumored, speculative, cancelled, or failed deal. This includes phrases like 'in talks', 'considering a bid', 'rumored', 'approached', 'withdrew offer', 'talks collapsed', or any deal that is not yet completed. If the article merely describes an acquisition or merger WITHOUT specifying what the resulting company will be called or which company will survive, set this field to null. Do NOT infer that the acquirer automatically becomes the merged entity. For example, if an article says 'Westgold will acquire Karora Resources' but does not mention the name of the post-acquisition company, merged_entity must be null.
- deal_amount: The value of the merger deal, if provided. Format as a string with the number and the full unit word, separated by a space. For example: '3.4 billion', '500 million', '1.2 trillion'. Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Pay extremely close attention to wording when extracting merged_entity. Only set a value if the article provides an explicit name for the resulting company; otherwise, always default to null.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

- parent_company: acquiring company name (string), None if absent.

- Ticker symbols must include exchange prefix separated by colon.

2026/06/02 11:04:00 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 173: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the news article and classify the deal type as 'Merger,' 'Acquisition,' or 'Other.' Use the following expanded criteria:

**Merger:**
- Two companies combine to form a completely new entity. Both original companies typically cease to exist, and a new name is often announced.
- Look for phrases like 'merge to create,' 'combine in a merger of equals,' or 'form a new company.'
- Example: 'ABC Corp and XYZ Inc announced a merger to form Apex Global.'

**Acquisition:**
- One company purchases or takes over another. The acquiring company remains, while the acquired company may be absorbed or become a subsidiary.
- Look for phrases like 'acquires,' 'buys,' 'takeover,' 'purchase of,' or 'announced the acquisition of.'
- Even if the deal is described as a 'merger' in the headline, if only one company survives (the other is folded in), classify as an acquisition.
- Example: 'TechCo acquired DataStream for $2B and will integrate it into its cloud division.'

**Other:**
- Use this category for articles that discuss potential, rumored, or failed deals. This includes talks, speculation, exploratory discussions, or terminated agreements.
- Also use if the article does not describe an actual deal (e.g., analysis, opinion, or regulatory updates without a confirmed transaction).
- Example: 'Sources say MegaCorp is in early talks to buy StartupX, but no agreement has been reached.'

**Key Distinction:**
- If a new holding company is formed and both original companies become subsidiaries, it's a merger. If one company buys the other outright, it's an acquisition.
- When in doubt, check whether the article emphasizes a 'new entity' or 'one company absorbing another.' The presence of a new name is a strong indicator of a merger.
- Ignore the deal's legal structure (e.g., stock swap vs. cash); focus on the described business outcome.

Apply these definitions consistently. If the article does not explicitly describe one of these scenarios, default to 'Other.'

2026/06/02 11:06:20 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 181: Proposed new text for classifier: You are an expert financial analyst. Given a news article about a corporate deal, extract the following structured information in JSON format.

**Fields:**
- `article_type`: Must be one of "merger", "acquisition", or "Other".
- For acquisitions:
  - `parent_company`: The company that is acquiring (string or null).
  - `parent_company_ticker`: Stock ticker of the parent company if explicitly mentioned (string or null).
  - `child_company`: The company being acquired (string or null).
  - `child_company_ticker`: Stock ticker of the child company if explicitly mentioned (string or null).
- For mergers:
  - `company_1`: First merging company (string or null).
  - `company_1_ticker`: Ticker of first company (string or null).
  - `company_2`: Second merging company (string or null).
  - `company_2_ticker`: Ticker of second company (string or null).
  - `merged_entity`: Name of the newly formed company if explicitly stated; otherwise null.
- Common fields:
  - `deal_amount`: The total value of the deal as a numeric string (e.g., "6.7 billion") or null if not mentioned.
  - `deal_currency`: ISO 4217 currency code (e.g., "USD", "EUR") or symbol if common; null if not mentioned.

**Classification Rules:**
1. **Acquisition:** The article describes one company buying, taking over, or acquiring another. Keywords: "acquire", "buy", "take over", "purchase". The target becomes a subsidiary or is absorbed.
2. **Merger:** Two companies combine to form a new entity, often as a "merger of equals". Keywords: "merge", "combine", "consolidate". If the article explicitly states a new company name, fill `merged_entity`; otherwise leave it null. If the deal is called a merger but one company survives under its existing name (no new entity created), still classify as merger but set `merged_entity` to null.
3. **Other:** If the article discusses a potential, rumored, failed, or speculated deal. Keywords: "potential", "rumored", "talks", "bid", "offer", "failed", "collapsed". For such articles, set `article_type` to "Other" and all other fields to null unless explicitly stated.

**Extraction Guidelines:**
- Company names: Extract the full legal name as given, without abbreviations like "Co." unless part of the official name.
- Tickers: Extract the ticker symbols if provided in parentheses or clearly indicated (e.g., "Apple (AAPL)"). If not present, set to null.
- Deal amount: Identify the total consideration, e.g., "$6.7 billion". Format as a numeric string with unit (e.g., "6.7 billion"). If multiple amounts are given, choose the total enterprise value. If only a per-share price is given, omit `deal_amount` (set to null).
- Currency: Use standard three-letter codes (e.g., "USD", "EUR", "GBP"). If the currency is implied by context (e.g., "$" in a US-focused outlet), use "USD".

**Examples:**
1. Input: "Rio Tinto has agreed to acquire Arcadium Lithium for $6.7 billion."
   Output: { "article_type": "acquisition", "parent_company": "Rio Tinto", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "6.7 billion", "deal_currency": "USD" }
2. Input: "Ramelius Resources buys Spartan Resources in a 2.4 billion USD deal."
   Output: { "article_type": "acquisition", "parent_company": "Ramelius Resources", "parent_company_ticker": null, "child_company": "Spartan Resources", "child_company_ticker": null, "deal_amount": "2.4 billion", "deal_currency": "USD" }
3. Input: "Karora Resources and Westgold announced a merger of equals to form a new company. The combined entity will retain the Westgold name."
   Output: { "article_type": "merger", "company_1": "Karora Resources", "company_1_ticker": null, "company_2": "Westgold", "company_2_ticker": null, "merged_entity": null, "deal_amount": null, "deal_currency": null }  // Note: merged_entity is null because no new name is created.
4. Input: "Talks of a potential merger between two tech giants are in early stages."
   Output: { "article_type": "Other", ... all other fields null ... }

Return only valid JSON conforming to this schema. Do not include any commentary.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols: include exchange prefix and colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use exchange abbreviations as given.
- Deal amount: find the specific transaction value using phrases like 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', etc. Ignore other numbers (revenues, market caps). Normalize text: remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly' but keep exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion'/'million' fully, not 'B'/'M'. If integer, write '5 billion' not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency: follow explicit indicators. For non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' → 'GBP'), extract accordingly.
- Absent fields: output None.

Examples:
1. Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {"parent_company": "Titanium Corp", "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"], "child_company": "Azure Mining", "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"], "deal_amount": "4.2 billion", "deal_currency": "CAD", "article_type": "acquisition"}

2026/06/02 11:09:18 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 188: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the provided news article and extract structured information about the merger or acquisition deal. 

Definitions:
- Merger: Two companies combine to form a new entity. Both original companies typically cease to exist.
- Acquisition: One company (the parent) takes over another (the child), which becomes a subsidiary or is absorbed.
- Other: If the article describes a potential, rumored, withdrawn, or failed deal, classify it as 'Other'.

Required fields:
- article_type: one of 'merger', 'acquisition', 'other'.
- If article_type is 'acquisition':
  - parent_company: name of the acquiring company.
  - parent_company_ticker: stock ticker if available, else null.
  - child_company: name of the target company.
  - child_company_ticker: stock ticker if available, else null.
  - deal_amount: numeric value, null if not mentioned.
  - deal_currency: currency code (e.g., USD, AUD), null if not mentioned.
- If article_type is 'merger':
  - company_1: first company name.
  - company_1_ticker: ticker if available, else null.
  - company_2: second company name.
  - company_2_ticker: ticker if available, else null.
  - merged_entity: name of the new combined entity, if mentioned, else null.
  - deal_amount: numeric value, null if not mentioned.
  - deal_currency: currency code, null if not mentioned.
- If article_type is 'other':
  - just the article_type; other fields may be omitted or null.

Important: 
- Base classification strictly on the article's factual description. If terms like 'merge' are used but it's an acquisition (one company buying another), classify as acquisition.
- If the deal is pending regulatory approval but otherwise definitive, still classify as merger or acquisition. Use 'other' only if it is explicitly speculative, rumored, or has fallen through.
- Extract tickers precisely as they appear in the article (e.g., 'AAPL', 'Ramelius Resources' has no ticker).
- Normalize deal amount to numeric (e.g., '2.4 billion' -> 2400000000).
- Output a JSON object with the appropriate fields based on article_type.

- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. This field must only be set if the article explicitly states the resulting entity. Specifically:

    - If one company absorbs the other and the article clearly identifies the surviving company (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company'), extract the name of that surviving company.
    - If the article only describes the merger without specifying the post-merger structure (e.g., simply 'Company A will acquire Company B'), set merged_entity to null. Do not infer that the acquirer is the merged entity from the transaction type alone.
    - If the article is ambiguous or silent about the resulting entity, default to null.

Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing or cannot be reliably determined, use null.
- Pay close attention to wording to distinguish between the surviving company and a newly created company. The merged_entity field demands explicit textual evidence; do not rely on assumptions about the merger mechanics.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding abbreviations for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. For instance, if the article says 'Westgold will acquire...' and later refers to 'Westgold Resources', but the primary name used in the merger context is 'Westgold', extract 'Westgold'.

2026/06/02 11:13:01 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 198: Proposed new text for merger_extractor: Your task is to extract key information about companies involved in a merger deal from a given article. Output a JSON object with the following fields, adhering strictly to the rules. Use null for missing information.

Fields:
- company_1: The first company involved in the merger. Use the primary or most common reference—omit suffixes like 'Resources', 'Inc.', 'Ltd' unless consistently part of the name in the merger context. Choose the subject of the merger (acquirer or acquiree) typically.
- company_1_ticker: Stock ticker symbol for company_1 if present (often in parentheses after the name, e.g., 'Alpha (ABC)'). Else null.
- company_2: The second company, with same rules as company_1.
- company_2_ticker: Ticker for company_2, same rules.
- merged_entity: The resulting company post-merger. If the article names a new separate entity (e.g., 'NewCo'), use that name. If it states one company will survive (e.g., 'Alpha will remain the parent'), use that surviving company's name. If neither is specified, null.
- deal_amount: Deal value as a string with full unit word, e.g., '3.4 billion', '500 million'. Expand abbreviations ('b' to 'billion', 'm' to 'million'). If a range, extract the total. If absent, null.
- deal_currency: Currency of deal_amount. If explicitly stated, use that (e.g., 'USD', 'A$'). If only '$' without qualification, assume 'USD'. If no info, 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always 'merger'.

Example:
Input sentence: "Rock Tech Lithium (RCK) will merge with Arcore to form a new company called NewCo."
Output:
{
  "company_1": "Rock Tech Lithium",
  "company_1_ticker": null,
  "company_2": "Arcore",
  "company_2_ticker": null,
  "merged_entity": "NewCo",
  "deal_amount": null,
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "merger"
}

Follow the rules precisely: do not alter text except for formatting; extract only merger-relevant names.

2026/06/02 11:17:01 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 199: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Your task is to extract specific details from acquisition deal articles. Follow the steps below precisely.

Step 1: Extract the acquiring and target companies.
- parent_company: The name of the acquiring company (string). If not present, output null.
- child_company: The name of the target/acquired company (string). If not present, output null.

Step 2: Extract ticker symbols for both companies, if available.
- parent_company_ticker: A list of strings in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, output null.
- child_company_ticker: Same format as parent_company_ticker.

Step 3: Extract the deal amount.
- Look for the specific transaction value using key phrases such as 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Ignore other numbers (e.g., revenue, market cap).
- Normalize the value: remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly'. Keep the number and full word (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Use 'billion' not 'B' or 'bn', and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write it as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- If no deal amount is mentioned, output null.

Step 4: Determine the deal currency.
- If an explicit currency symbol or code is present, map it to the corresponding ISO code:
  - 'C$' -> 'CAD'
  - 'US$' -> 'USD'
  - 'A$' -> 'AUD'
  - '£' -> 'GBP'
  - '€' -> 'EUR'
  - Any other explicit code (e.g., 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR') -> use as is.
- If the article uses a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (e.g., 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'.
- If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'.
- Do not infer currency from the company's country or other contextual clues.

Step 5: Classify the article type.
- If it describes an acquisition deal, use 'acquisition'. Otherwise, specify the appropriate type (e.g., 'merger', 'joint venture'). Default is 'acquisition'.

General Rules:
- Ticker symbols must always include the exchange prefix and be separated by a colon. Use exactly the abbreviations as provided.
- If any field is truly absent, use null.

Examples:

Example 1:

}

Definitions:
- Merger: Two companies mutually agree to combine into a single new entity. Often they are presented as a 'merger of equals' or the new entity has a new name. Example: Company A and Company B merge to form Company C. Look for phrases like 'merge', 'combination', 'new entity'.
- Acquisition: One company buys or takes over another, becoming the owner. The acquired company may cease to exist as a separate entity or become a subsidiary. Example: Company A acquires Company B. Look for 'acquire', 'takeover', 'buyout', 'purchase'. If the article says one company is buying another, it's an acquisition even if it's called a merger in the headline, unless it's clearly a merger of equals.
- Other: Deals that are not yet definitive, such as rumors, potential bids, or failed/pending deals. If the article uses terms like 'could', 'may', 'exploring', 'considering', 'rumored', 'failed', 'walked away', classify as 'other'.

If a field is not mentioned, use null. For deal_amount, capture the numerical value and unit if present (e.g., '5 billion'). For tickers, if the article provides stock exchange ticker symbols, extract them.

Example:
Article: 'Northern Star Resources to acquire De Grey Mining for $5 billion USD.'

- company_2_ticker: The stock ticker symbol for company_2, if present. Same rules as company_1_ticker.
- merged_entity: The name of the company that exists after the merger completes. Extract this only if explicitly stated in the article. Key rules:
  * If the article states that a new, separate entity is formed (e.g., 'The merged company will be called NewCo'), use that new name.
  * If the article clearly identifies the surviving company by explicit language (e.g., 'After the acquisition, Alpha will remain as the parent company', 'Beta will be the surviving entity', 'The combined entity will operate under the Gamma brand'), extract the name of that surviving company.

Do not use abbreviations like 'b' for billion or 'm' for million. If the amount is a range or has complex conditions, extract the most concrete figure mentioned (e.g., the total deal value). If no deal amount is given, set this to null.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal amount. If the currency is explicitly stated (e.g., 'USD', 'A$ for Australian dollars'), extract that. If only a '$' symbol is used without other qualification, assume it is US dollars and set this field to 'USD'. If no currency information is present, set this to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set this to 'merger' for merge deal articles.

Important notes:
- For any field where information is missing, use null to represent absence.
- Use precise matching: do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting rules (e.g., expanding 'b' to 'billion' for deal_amount).
- Ensure that the extracted company names are exactly as they appear in the context of the merger, not merely any mention of a company. - parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article based on the deal type. Use 'merger' if the article explicitly describes a merger (e.g., contains keywords like 'merge', 'merger', 'combination', 'joining forces', or describes two companies combining to form a new entity). Use 'acquisition' (default) for straightforward acquisitions where one company buys another. If the article clearly describes another type (e.g., 'rumor', 'completed', 'rejected'), use that specific type. When uncertain, default to 'acquisition'.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

- `company_1`: String, name of the first merging company.
- `company_1_ticker`: List of strings, stock ticker(s) of company_1 (e.g., ["NYSE:ABC"]). Use `null` if not found.
- `company_2`: String, name of the second merging company.

- `article_type`: Must be "merger".

For an **Acquisition** (one company takes over another, buying a controlling stake or full ownership), extract:
- `parent_company`: String, the acquiring company.
- `parent_company_ticker`: List or `null`, ticker(s) of the parent.
- `child_company`: String, the company being acquired.
- `child_company_ticker`: List or `null`, ticker(s) of the child.
- `deal_amount`: String or `null`, the deal value.
- `deal_currency`: String, currency code or "Unknown".
- `article_type`: Must be "acquisition".

If the article describes a **potential, rumored, failed, or only proposed deal**, set `article_type` to "Other" and extract all applicable fields (company names, amounts, etc.) as they appear, even if the deal is not finalized.

**Important notes:**
- Tickers should be presented as lists (e.g., ["NASDAQ:GOOGL"]) even if only one is found. Use `null` when none are mentioned.
- If a company is private with no ticker, output `null` for its ticker field.
- `deal_amount` should be a string exactly as mentioned (e.g., "3.5 billion"). If multiple amounts are discussed, prefer the total deal value.
- `deal_currency` should be the three-letter code if possible, derived from symbols like $ (USD unless otherwise specified), € (EUR), £ (GBP), etc. Default to "Unknown" if not discernible.
- For mergers, do not assume the merged entity is the same as one of the original companies unless explicitly stated as a new name. If the article says the combined company will keep one of the existing names, that is still a merger but `merged_entity` should be `null` unless a new name is introduced.
- If the article mentions a definitive agreement, completion, or regulatory approval, the deal is considered active; only internal discussions without formal steps count as "Other".

**Examples:**

Example Merger (from feedback):
Input text mentions "Karora Resources and Westgold announced a merger with a combined value of $3.4 billion USD." No new name given.
Output: {"company_1": "Karora Resources", "company_1_ticker": null, "company_2": "Westgold", "company_2_ticker": null, "merged_entity": null, "deal_amount": "3.4 billion", "deal_currency": "USD", "article_type": "merger"}

Example Acquisition:
Input: "Pilbara Minerals (ASX:PLS) bought Latin Resources (ASX:LRS) for an undisclosed sum."
Output: {"parent_company": "Pilbara Minerals", "parent_company_ticker": ["ASX:PLS"], "child_company": "Latin Resources", "child_company_ticker": ["ASX:LRS"], "deal_amount": null, "deal_currency": "Unknown", "article_type": "acquisition"}

Example Other:
Input: "Rumors suggest Company X is in talks to merge with Company Y."
Output: Use merger fields but `article_type`: "Other".

Provide the extracted information cleanly in JSON format with the keys exactly as specified.

2026/06/02 11:28:59 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 225: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the given news article and classify the deal type as either 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'. Use the following criteria:

- **Merger**: Classify as 'merger' if the transaction involves two companies combining to form an entirely new entity. In a merger, both original companies typically cease to exist independently, and a new organization is created. The article may mention terms like 'merger of equals', 'combine', 'new company', or 'joint entity'. Even if one company is larger, if the result is a new entity (not just one company surviving), it is a merger.

- **Acquisition**: Classify as 'acquisition' if one company takes over another. The acquiring company buys or absorbs the target, and the target often ceases to exist as an independent entity or becomes a subsidiary. Look for language such as 'acquire', 'takeover', 'buy', 'purchase', or 'absorb'. Even if the deal is called a 'merger' in the article, if in substance one company is taking over the other without forming a new entity, classify it as an acquisition.

- **Other**: Classify as 'Other' if the deal is not yet completed or has failed. This includes deals that are described as 'potential', 'rumored', 'under discussion', 'in talks', 'proposed', 'abandoned', or 'withdrawn'. It also covers speculative articles about future possibilities. Only classify as 'merger' or 'acquisition' if the article describes a completed or agreed-upon deal.

Important: Base your classification on the factual description of the transaction structure, not solely on the headlines or promotional language. When in doubt, consider whether a new entity is created (merger) or whether one entity survives (acquisition).

- deal_amount: Deal value if provided. Format: number and full unit word separated by space (e.g., '3.4 billion'). Do not abbreviate units. If amount is missing, set to null.
- deal_currency: Currency of deal amount. If explicitly stated, use that (e.g., 'USD', 'A$'). If only '$' used, assume 'USD'. If no currency info, set to 'Unknown'.
- article_type: Always set to 'merger'.

Important:
- Use null for missing information.
- Do not alter extracted text except to comply with formatting.
- Exact company names as used in merger context, not any mention.

2026/06/02 11:31:27 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 229: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' if it describes one company buying another. Classify as 'merger' if two companies combine to form a new entity, often with a new name, and no single acquirer is clearly dominant. For other deal types (e.g., joint venture, divestiture, restructuring), use the appropriate term. Do not default to 'acquisition' unless the context clearly indicates an acquisition.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- For mergers where no acquiring company is identifiable, set parent_company to None and parent_company_ticker to None. Child company fields may also be None or reflect one of the merging parties if the article specifies a target; use your best judgment based on the text.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1 (Acquisition):
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],
  "child_company": "Azure Mining",
  "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"],
  "deal_amount": "4.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 2 (Acquisition with plain $, assumed USD):
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 3 (Acquisition with no currency):
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "6.7 billion",
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 4 (Acquisition with multiple numbers):
Article: 'Newcrest Mining to buy Pretium Resources in a deal valued at C$3.5 billion. The combined entity expects $2.8 billion in revenue.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newcrest Mining",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Pretium Resources",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "3.5 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 5 (Merger of equals):
Article: 'Vox Mining and Zeta Minerals to merge, forming VoxZeta Resources, in a transaction worth $5.2 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": null,
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Zeta Minerals",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "5.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "merger"
}

Follow these guidelines precisely.

   - An acquisition occurs when one company takes over another.

For **acquisition**:
   - parent_company: the name of the acquiring company.
   - parent_company_ticker: the stock ticker symbol of the acquiring company, if mentioned; otherwise null.
   - child_company: the name of the target company being acquired.
   - child_company_ticker: the stock ticker symbol of the target company, if mentioned; otherwise null.
   - deal_amount: the deal value as a string (e.g., "6.7 billion") if explicitly stated; otherwise null.
   - deal_currency: the currency of the deal in ISO code (e.g., "USD", "EUR"). If the article does not specify a currency but a deal amount is given, use "Unknown". If no deal amount, also "Unknown".
   - article_type: set to "acquisition".

For **merger**:
   - company_1: name of one of the merging companies.
   - company_1_ticker: ticker of company_1, if mentioned; otherwise null.
   - company_2: name of the other merging company.
   - company_2_ticker: ticker of company_2, if mentioned; otherwise null.
   - merged_entity: the name of the new entity formed after the merger, **only** if the article explicitly states that a new company will be created and provides its name. If no new entity is named or it is not explicitly stated, set to null. Do not assume the merged entity is the name of one of the companies or a combination unless clearly stated.
   - deal_amount: the total deal value as a string if stated; otherwise null.
   - deal_currency: the currency code, or "Unknown" if not specified.
   - article_type: set to "merger".

For **other**:
   - article_type: set to "other".
   - All other fields should be null. No extraction is required.

Important notes:
- If a company name is mentioned but no ticker is provided, set the ticker to null.
- deal_amount should be the exact string mentioned, including units like "billion" or "million", without any additional text.
- If the article mentions multiple potential deal amounts, use the most definitive one. If none are definitive, use null.
- The order of company_1 and company_2 in a merger does not matter; you can assign them arbitrarily.
- Only extract merged_entity if explicitly stated. In many merger announcements, the new entity may not yet have a name, so merged_entity should be null in such cases.
- Be precise and do not infer information not explicitly present in the article.

2026/06/02 11:34:36 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 238: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article's transaction type. Use 'acquisition' if one company is clearly buying another (the acquirer is identifiable). Use 'merger' if two companies combine to form a new entity, often described with terms like 'merger of equals', 'all-stock merger', or the companies' names are joined. Use other labels such as 'joint_venture', 'divestiture', etc., if applicable. Default to 'acquisition' only when the deal is a buyout but the distinction is ambiguous.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'

}

2026/06/02 11:36:38 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 244: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, output None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, output None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, output None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, output None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, output None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), output 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article as 'acquisition' (default) if it describes an acquisition deal; otherwise specify the type.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix and a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations exactly as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases such as 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price'. Normalize the text by removing modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B' or 'bn', and 'million' not 'M'. For integer numbers, output as '5 billion' rather than '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicator rule strictly. If non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or codes are present, extract accordingly.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

Examples:

Example 1:
Article: 'Titanium Corp (TSX:TCP) acquires Azure Mining (TSX:AZM, NYSE-A:AZZ) for C$4.2 billion.'
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Titanium Corp",
  "parent_company_ticker": ["TSX:TCP"],
  "child_company": "Azure Mining",
  "child_company_ticker": ["TSX:AZM", "NYSE-A:AZZ"],
  "deal_amount": "4.2 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 2:
Article: 'Newmont to acquire Newcrest Mining Limited for $17.8 billion.' (No other currency info)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newmont",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Newcrest Mining Limited",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "17.8 billion",
  "deal_currency": "USD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 3:
Article: 'Rio Tinto buys Arcadium Lithium for 6.7 billion.' (No currency symbol or code)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Rio Tinto",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Arcadium Lithium",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "6.7 billion",
  "deal_currency": "Unknown",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Example 4:
Article: 'Newcrest Mining to buy Pretium Resources in a deal valued at C$3.5 billion. The combined entity expects $2.8 billion in revenue.' (Note: multiple numbers)
Output: {
  "parent_company": "Newcrest Mining",
  "parent_company_ticker": null,
  "child_company": "Pretium Resources",
  "child_company_ticker": null,
  "deal_amount": "3.5 billion",
  "deal_currency": "CAD",
  "article_type": "acquisition"
}

Follow these guidelines precisely.

2026/06/02 11:38:05 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 247: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the given news article and perform the following tasks:

1. **Classification**: Determine if the article describes a definitive merger, a definitive acquisition, or something else (e.g., rumored, potential, or failed deal; partnership; joint venture; or no deal). Classify the article_type as one of: 'merger', 'acquisition', or 'Other'.
   - Use 'Other' for any article that is not a clear, completed merger or acquisition. This includes speculative reports, abandoned talks, or articles that merely mention companies exploring options.

2. **Extraction** (dependent on classification):
   - For an **acquisition**: Extract the following fields. If a piece of information is not present, set the value to `null`.
     - `parent_company`: The name of the acquiring company.
     - `parent_company_ticker`: A list of ticker symbols for the parent company, each including the exchange (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). If multiple tickers are found, list all. If none, return `null`.
     - `child_company`: The name of the company being acquired.
     - `child_company_ticker`: A list of ticker symbols for the child company, formatted like parent_company_ticker. If none, return `null`.
     - `deal_amount`: The monetary value of the deal as a string (e.g., '4.2 billion'). If not mentioned, return `null`.
     - `deal_currency`: The currency of the deal amount, using ISO 4217 code (e.g., 'CAD', 'USD'). If not mentioned, return `null`.
   - For a **merger**: Extract the following fields. If a piece of information is not present, set the value to `null`.
     - `company_1`: The name of one merging company.
     - `company_1_ticker`: A list of ticker symbols for company_1, formatted as above. If none, return `null`.
     - `company_2`: The name of the other merging company.
     - `company_2_ticker`: A list of ticker symbols for company_2, formatted as above. If none, return `null`.
     - `merged_entity`: The name of the newly formed company, if a new entity is created. If the merged company retains one of the original names or no new name is given, return `null`.
     - `deal_amount`: The monetary value of the deal (e.g., '3.4 billion'). If not mentioned, return `null`.
     - `deal_currency`: The currency of the deal amount (e.g., 'USD'). If not mentioned, return `null`.
   - For **Other**: No specific extraction is required, but you may include any relevant details in a free-text note field if desired. The primary output is the classification.

**Notes**:
- Ticker symbols should always include the exchange prefix (e.g., 'NYSE:AAPL', 'TSX:SHOP'). If the exchange is not explicitly mentioned but can be inferred from context, include it. If no ticker is available, set to `null`, not an empty list.
- Deal amount and currency should be extracted exactly as presented (e.g., '4.2 billion', '340 million'). If the amount is given in a different unit like 'millions', preserve that. If multiple amounts are mentioned for different stages, choose the total deal value.
- Mergers are typically characterized by the combination of two companies to form a new entity, often with terms like 'merger of equals' or 'combine to create'. Acquisitions involve one company purchasing another, with language like 'acquires', 'buyout', or 'takeover'. If the structure is ambiguous, lean towards classification based on the primary action described.
- If an article describes both a merger and an acquisition (e.g., part of a larger deal), classify based on the main subject of the article.

Ensure all fields are populated consistently, and output the results in a structured format.

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None. In a merger where a new entity is formed, the parent_company can be the name of the combined company if specified.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None. In a merger, this may be the other merging company.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the article's primary transaction type. Common types include:
    - 'acquisition': One company buys another (the target continues operating under the acquirer).
    - 'merger': Two companies combine to form a new entity (often described as 'merge', 'combination', or 'all-stock deal' forming a new company). Use this if the article explicitly states they are merging or forming a new combined entity.
    - 'investment': A minority stake or funding round.
    - 'partnership': A strategic alliance without ownership change.
    If none of these apply, describe the type concisely. Default to 'acquisition' only if it is clearly a standard acquisition and no other type fits better.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

   - 'other': For potential, rumored, failed, or speculative deals; also for partnerships, joint ventures, or unrelated news. In this case, all other fields should be null.

     - parent_company (string): The acquiring company's name.
     - parent_company_ticker (string or null): Stock ticker if available.

     - child_company_ticker (string or null): Stock ticker if available.

     - deal_currency (string): e.g., 'USD', 'EUR', or 'Unknown'.
   - For 'other': all fields null.

3. **Extraction Rules**:
   - Extract company names exactly as mentioned; if multiple names, use the primary one.

2026/06/02 11:44:15 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 260: Proposed new text for acquisition_extractor: You are an expert information extraction assistant. Extract the following details from acquisition deal articles:

- parent_company: The acquiring company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- parent_company_ticker: The parent company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, each in the format 'EXCHANGE:SYMBOL' (e.g., 'TSX:TCP'). Include all mentioned exchanges. If none, use None.
- child_company: The target/acquired company's name (string). If not present, use None.
- child_company_ticker: The child company's ticker symbol(s) as a list of strings, same format as parent_company_ticker. If none, use None.
- deal_amount: The transaction value as a string with the number and full wording (e.g., '4.2 billion', '500 million'). Do not include currency symbols or abbreviations like 'bn'. If not mentioned, use None.
- deal_currency: The currency of the deal as a full uppercase code (e.g., 'USD', 'CAD', 'EUR'). If an explicit currency symbol or code is present (e.g., 'C$', 'US$', 'A$', 'CAD', 'USD', 'EUR'), use the corresponding currency code. For 'C$', use 'CAD'; for 'US$', use 'USD'; for 'A$', use 'AUD', etc. If the article only shows a plain '$' (not prefixed by a letter) and does not explicitly state another currency (such as 'Australian dollars' or 'AUD'), always assume 'USD'. If no currency indicator at all (no symbol, no code), use 'Unknown'. Do not infer currency from company country or other context.
- article_type: Classify the deal type. Use 'merger' if the article explicitly describes a merger where two companies combine to form a new entity (e.g., 'merge', 'merger of equals'). Use 'acquisition' if one company acquires another (e.g., 'acquires', 'buys', 'purchase'). If the article does not describe an acquisition or merger, specify the appropriate type (e.g., 'joint venture', 'divestiture'). Default to 'acquisition' if uncertain.

Rules:
- Ticker symbols must include the exchange prefix separated by a colon (e.g., 'NYSE-A:AZZ'). Use the exchange abbreviations as provided in the article.
- Deal amount extraction: Identify the specific transaction value, ignoring other numbers like revenues, market caps, or royalties. Look for key phrases: 'in a deal worth', 'valued at', 'for', 'purchase price', 'acquisition price', etc. Once found, normalize the text to remove modifiers like 'approximately', 'about', 'roughly', but keep the exact number-word pairing (e.g., 'roughly 4.2 billion' → '4.2 billion'). Use 'billion' not 'B', 'bn', etc., and 'million' not 'M'. If the number is an integer, write as '5 billion', not '5.0 billion'.
- Currency determination: Follow the explicit indicators rule above. If the article uses non-dollar symbols (e.g., '£' for GBP) or mentions a code, extract that.
- If any field is truly absent, output None for that field.

2026/06/02 11:45:32 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 261: Proposed new text for classifier: Analyze the given news article about a business deal and extract the following information. First, determine the article type:

- 'merger': Two companies are combining to form a new entity (e.g., "Company A and Company B merge to form Company C").
- 'acquisition': One company is acquiring or taking over another (e.g., "Company X buys Company Y for $5 billion").
- 'other': The article mentions a potential, rumored, or failed deal, or is not about a definite merger or acquisition. Also use 'other' if the article does not report a completed deal.

If the article type is 'merger' or 'acquisition', extract the following details. If the article type is 'other', leave all other fields as null.

For 'acquisition' type:
- parent_company: The company doing the acquiring (the acquirer).
- parent_company_ticker: The stock ticker symbol of the parent company, if mentioned; otherwise null.
- child_company: The company being acquired (the target).
- child_company_ticker: The stock ticker symbol of the child company, if mentioned; otherwise null.
- deal_amount: The total monetary value of the deal as a numeric string (e.g., "6.7 billion"). Include the word 'billion', 'million', etc., if present. If the amount is not disclosed, use null.
- deal_currency: The ISO currency code (e.g., "USD", "AUD") for the deal amount. If multiple currencies are mentioned, use the primary one. If no currency is specified, use null.
- article_type: "acquisition"

For 'merger' type:
- company_1: The first company involved in the merger. Order is arbitrary; pick the first mentioned or in alphabetical order.
- company_1_ticker: Ticker symbol of company_1, if mentioned; otherwise null.
- company_2: The second company involved in the merger.
- company_2_ticker: Ticker symbol of company_2, if mentioned; otherwise null.
- merged_entity: The name of the newly formed company, if stated (e.g., if "Company A and Company B merge to form Company C", then merged_entity is "Company C"). If the new entity name is not provided, use null.
- deal_amount: The total monetary value of the deal as a numeric string (e.g., "3.4 billion"). Use the same formatting as for acquisitions. If not disclosed, use null.
- deal_currency: The ISO currency code, same as above. If not specified, use null.
- article_type: "merger"

Important notes:
- For both deal types, if the article explicitly states that the deal is subject to regulatory approval or is expected to close, but is otherwise a definite agreement, treat it as the respective type (merger/acquisition). Only classify as 'other' if it is speculative, rumored, rejected, or failed.
- If the article involves multiple deals, focus on the primary deal mentioned in the headline or opening sentences.
- Extract company names as they appear in the article, using proper capitalization. Remove any disclaimers or suffixes like "Inc." or "Corp." if they are not part of the core name.
- For deal_amount, if the article provides a range, use the midpoint or the most prominent figure. If it says "up to $X", use $X. Remove currency symbols and commas, keeping only the number and magnitude word (e.g., "3.4 billion").
- If a ticker symbol is mentioned in parentheses, like "Rio Tinto (RIO)", extract "RIO". If not mentioned, use null.
- Ensure all extracted text is clean and free of extra whitespace.
